# Review ledger: workflow-hardening review

Transient working state for the review loop. Separate from the plan; deleted at task close. Committed so it survives context loss and travels across sessions.

Task: independently review the workflow-hardening changes (commits `8ce9454`, `1d15f22`, `8f03a9c`; diff range `f2ca59e..8f03a9c`) for correctness, quality, and consistency with the project's principles, then address valid findings.

Interrupt requests (received during this task, to fold into the plan via the planner, not done ad hoc):

- `human-onboarding`: add a "Getting started, for the human" section to the canonical `AGENTS.md` with one inline editable kickoff prompt, plus a brief README mirror pointing to it (approach B + thin C). Decided by the human.
- `ledger-template`: make the review ledger a scaffolded template asset (lean: a reference asset under `.agents/`, copied per task), so the ledger schema is pinned rather than invented ad hoc. Recommended; human leaning yes.

- `deliberation-mode`: acknowledge question-driven (Socratic) human input as a first-class entry mode; generalise the intake / clarifying-questions / Open-Questions machinery so the orchestrator answers a human question with options, trade-offs, a recommendation, and reasoning (human decides); bind each resolved question to a durable Open-Questions decision; define convergence as the human committing a decision to action, guarded by the existing "no re-raise without new evidence" rule. LOCKED by the human: approach B plus the thin C naming slice (not a new phase or role). Pairs with `human-onboarding`.
- `human-review-queue`: make the plan's "Open Questions, Decisions, Issues and Blockers" section a single living human-decision queue, each item with a stable id, a one-line ask, a status (open / decided -> folded into <step> / superseded) and a context pointer; resolved items marked resolved (not deleted) to prevent double-addressing; the orchestrator updates it at every checkpoint as a required step. Recommended (reuse Open Questions, no second artifact). Human-interface cluster with `deliberation-mode` and `human-onboarding`.

Also to fix in the planner pass: the plan's Open Questions section says "No open questions remain", which is stale (the convergence fix, the queued feature designs, and the 14 review findings are all open). Rewrite it as the living queue above.

All of these are non-trivial (touch the canonical pack assets), so they route to the planner in a single pass alongside the triager-valid review findings.

Human decisions (this checkpoint): single planner pass covering everything; `human-review-queue` = approach A (reuse the Open Questions section) upgraded with B's discipline (stable ids, explicit status, resolved-marked-not-deleted). Refinement: a new human would not know to check the queue, and a pull-only model is fragile, so the queue must be PUSH + pull: the orchestrator surfaces open items at each checkpoint, and `human-onboarding` documents the human's ongoing duty to decide on them. Planner (opus) dispatched to fold everything into the plan.

Planner returned. Plan now has six new Roadmap steps (`convergence-accounting`, `workflow-doc-fixes`, `human-onboarding`, `deliberation-mode`, `human-review-queue`, `ledger-template`), a rewritten living Open-Questions queue (Q-1..Q-10 with recommendations), and two added Success Criteria. F3 (the "not committed" contradiction) corrected in the plan's resume-anchor narrative immediately, rather than waiting for `workflow-doc-fixes`, since it would mislead a resuming agent. Next: plan review (independent reviewers on the updated plan), then the human resolves the open decisions to converge the plan, then implement. Open decisions pushed to the human: Q-1 (convergence-accounting, headline) plus Q-2..Q-7 (recommendation-backed); Q-8/Q-9/Q-10 already decided.

Human decided Q-1 = approach (b): drop the contested-rounds cap, keep a single total-round cap (default five). Marked decided in the plan queue. Q-2..Q-7 remain at their recommended defaults, awaiting confirmation. Round 2 = plan review: two independent reviewers (A plan-soundness, opus; B consistency/principles, sonnet) dispatched on the updated plan, told Q-1 = (b). Triager to follow.

NEW FINDING (found during implementation; needs a plan item): `include_dir!` in src/manifest.rs embeds pack/ but does NOT register those files as cargo rebuild dependencies, so `just scaffold-self` can regenerate the root AGENTS.md and `.agents/` from a STALE embedded pack. The bootstrap commit's committed `.agents/` copies were in fact stale (missing the hardening content); a forced `cargo clean -p agent-scaffold` + rebuild caught them up in this working tree. Impact: scaffold-self is unreliable without a forced rebuild, and the planned golden test would be fooled by the same staleness. Fix candidate: a build.rs emitting `cargo:rerun-if-changed=pack`. Route to the planner as a new step (e.g. `pack-rebuild-tracking`), prioritised before the golden test and before relying on scaffold-self. Surfaced to the human.

Human decision (triager structure): ALWAYS a separate triager, NEVER collapsed (Option 3). The triager must be independent of BOTH the producer AND the orchestrator (the orchestrator drives the loop and owns convergence/cost, so it is biased toward dismissing findings to converge), for every review round including trivial ones. Round 1: two independent reviewers dispatched (R1 correctness/termination lens, opus; R2 cross-document-consistency/principles lens, sonnet). ### Round 1 reviewer findings, pre-triage (R1: correctness / termination)

- R1-H1 (high). The recorded per-round outcome is binary (clean vs new valid findings), but the contested-rounds cap needs a third "contested" state that is never recorded or defined, and findings have no cross-round identity, so a re-spawned orchestrator cannot compute the contested cap from the ledger. (`orchestrator.md` steps 1-3; `AGENTS.md` convergence bullets and line ~136.)
- R1-H2 (high). The convergence taxonomy is not a partition: "new valid findings" and "still-contested valid findings" both describe non-clean rounds with no boundary, and the two caps use different numbers (3 vs 5) for identically recorded rounds, making the escalation trigger nondeterministic.
- R1-M1 (med). The dismissed-high-severity re-check keys on an undefined severity scale; it says "high-severity" but its stated purpose is catching a "critical" finding, which a critical-above-high scale would exclude. Severity levels are never enumerated in `reviewer.md`/`triager.md`.
- R1-M2 (med). The second-triager re-check is ordered after the round outcome is recorded, and the procedure never says what to do if the re-check overturns the dismissal (flip outcome, reset streak, refix). The blocking relationship is implied, not a step.
- R1-M3 (med). Acceptance review is described "as in the other review phases" (implying the convergence loop) but the README diagram draws it as a single pass with no loop, cap, or escalation. Prose and diagram disagree.
- R1-M4 (med). Off-by-one between prose ("exceed" / "past" / "after") and the diagram ("cap hit"); the boundary phrasings resolve to different round numbers.
- R1-M5 (med). Counts are per-artifact but the ledger is per-task; no rule resets the consecutive-clean count and round total when moving to a new artifact/step, so a re-spawned orchestrator must infer segmentation that is never specified.
- R1-M6 (med). The escalation-resume path does not reset the round counters, so a total-cap escalation can immediately re-fire on resume; the intended semantics are undefined.
- R1-L1 (low). "Risky / high-blast-radius" artifact classification is undefined yet selects the required consecutive-clean count (one vs two); subjective and not recorded.
- R1-L2 (low). The "clean round" definition ("every finding dismissed") does not explicitly cover the common zero-findings case.
- R1-L3 (low). The README diagram routes a trivial interrupt into the "implement the next step" node, a dead-end when no steps are pending, and mismatches the prose (the orchestrator folds a trivial request in directly).

### Round 1 reviewer findings, pre-triage (R2: consistency / principles, sonnet)

- R2-H1 (high). The plan doc (`docs/plans/agent-scaffold.md`, `core-assets` narrative) says the ledger is "a durable scratch file (not committed)", contradicting `AGENTS.md`, `orchestrator.md`, and `CHANGELOG.md` ("committed beside its plan"). In the resume-anchor doc, so it would mislead a resuming agent into the opposite design.
- R2-M1 (med). `AGENTS.md` convergence bullets list only the contested-rounds cap; the total-round cap lives in the separate "backstops" section, so an agent scanning the bullets as a checklist gets an incomplete escalation rule.
- R2-M2 (med). Acceptance "as in the other review phases" is ambiguous about whether the convergence loop applies; the diagram shows a single pass. [= R1-M3]
- R2-M3 (med). README intake caption "human decides routing" overstates: the prose has the orchestrator fold trivial requests in directly.
- R2-M4 (med). README `intake -> impl` for trivial interrupts is wrong when the interrupt arrives during plan review (no pending step). [= R1-L3]
- R2-M5 (med). `AGENTS.md` source line-wrap splits "the review / has converged" (cosmetic; Markdown soft-wrap, to be judged).
- R2-M6 (med). `CHANGELOG.md` line-wrap leaves "guarded" at a line end (cosmetic).
- R2-L1 (low). `AGENTS.md` convergence bullet 3 says "a bounded number of rounds" not "contested rounds", ambiguous against the total cap. [related R1-M4]
- R2-L2 (low). The plan calls them "three round constants" when one (required consecutive clean rounds) has two values.

### Combined unique findings F1-F17 handed to the triager

Merged (deduped): F6 = R1-M3 + R2-M2; F7 = R1-M4 + R2-L1; F12 = R1-L3 + R2-M4. F1/F2 (deep convergence-rule defects) kept distinct from F10 (bullet omits total cap). Triager (opus) dispatched to adjudicate all 17 against the current sources.

### Round 1 triager verdicts (14 valid, 3 invalid)

Valid, to fix: F1 (high); F2, F3, F4, F5, F6, F7, F9, F12 (medium); F8, F10, F15, F16, F17 (low). Invalid, dismissed: F11 (the diagram caption faithfully matches the prose); F13, F14 (cosmetic Markdown soft-wrap, renders as continuous text).

No high-severity finding was dismissed, so the dismissed-high-severity second-triager guard did not trigger. Round 1 outcome: NEW VALID FINDINGS (not a clean round); consecutive-clean stays 0.

Themes of the valid findings:

- Convergence accounting (F1 high, F2, F7, F10): the "contested" round state the contested-rounds cap depends on is never defined or recorded, the round taxonomy is not a partition, cap-boundary wording is inconsistent, and the bullets omit the total cap. Core defect; needs a design decision on the fix.
- Ledger/plan contradiction (F3): the plan says the ledger is "not committed" vs the prompts/CHANGELOG "committed beside its plan".
- Under-specified mechanics (F5 re-check overturn handling, F8 counter reset across artifacts, F9 escalation-resume counter reset, F15 risk classification, F16 zero-findings clean round).
- Severity scale undefined (F4): guard triggers on "high" but aims at "critical".
- Acceptance loop ambiguity (F6): "as in other phases" vs single-pass diagram.
- Diagram vs prose (F12): trivial-interrupt edge routes to impl, a dead-end pre-implementation.
- Nit (F17): "three round constants" imprecise (one has two values).

Next: single planner pass folding the 14 valid findings plus the three queued feature requests into the plan; then plan review, implement, round 2 review.

## Post-189b7bd notes (re-added; an implementer's fmt + git checkout clobbered these)

INCIDENT / lesson: the `workflow-doc-fixes` implementer ran repo-wide `just fmt` (which reformats this ledger and the plan as a side effect), then reverted the ledger with `git checkout docs/plans/agent-scaffold.ledger.md` to honour "do not touch the ledger". That reverted the ledger to HEAD (189b7bd), discarding the uncommitted Q-14 notes. Mitigations going forward: (a) commit the ledger before spawning an implementer, so its uncommitted state is not at risk; (b) implementers must NOT run repo-wide `just fmt` or `git checkout` on files they do not own (fmt only their files; leave incidental reformatting for the orchestrator). A real workflow finding (candidate plan item); reinforces committing the ledger frequently and the structured-record / findings-files work.

Q-14 (findings-to-files) DECIDED by the human: adopt. Reviewers and triagers write their findings to per-agent files that other agents read directly (parallel-safe); the ledger references them by path and keeps the decision trail; an orchestrator-owned cleanup step deletes them once the round is fully resolved or at task close. Compose with `ledger-template` (schema) and `state-schema`, one schema and validator. Reasoning: Principle 5 (removes the lossy transcription hop that dropped A-M5) and Principle 1 (findings file = single source, referenced not copied). Fold into the structured-record cluster at the next planner touch; non-blocking.

`workflow-doc-fixes` implemented (F4/F5/F6/F8/F9/F12/F15/F16/F17); regenerated, 46 tests/clippy/fmt clean, ASCII-clean; not committed. Reviewing it with TWO reviewers (A correctness/completeness, opus; B consistency/principles, sonnet), DOGFOODING Q-14: each reviewer WRITES its findings to a file under `docs/plans/agent-scaffold.reviews/`, and the separate triager reads those files directly (no orchestrator transcription). Files cleaned up at round close.

New human request (interrupt): queue the `optional-modules` agent-isolation work (agent-images / agent-box) to prevent the cross-agent file-conflict class (the git-checkout ledger clobber). Intake (per the human-input contract). Recommendation: DECOUPLE the incident from the isolation feature. (1) Now, fix the incident cheaply with discipline (implementers format only their own files, never `git checkout` files they do not own; the orchestrator commits the ledger before spawning writers) as a small workflow-rule plan item, sibling to `pack-rebuild-tracking`. (2) Pursue isolation deliberately for its real value (parallel implementation, sandboxing), NOT as the incident fix; when taken up, do WORKTREE isolation first (native to the harness, proportionate to the file-conflict class) and the heavier `agent-box` / `agent-images` CONTAINER isolation as a later follow-on (adds sandboxing/environment isolation, a separate motivation). Reasoning: Principle 2 (minimal; do not over-build the core for one incident), Principle 6 (evidence before heavy investment), Principle 1 (isolation is the cleaner structural answer, so pursue it, but deliberately and worktree-first). Awaiting the human's decision on queuing and priority.

`workflow-doc-fixes` review (Q-14 findings files, no orchestrator transcription):

- Reviewer A (correctness): 0/0/0/3L -> `docs/plans/agent-scaffold.reviews/workflow-doc-fixes-A.md`.
- Reviewer B (consistency): 0/1H/2M/3L -> `.../workflow-doc-fixes-B.md`. The high: `orchestrator.md` omits "acceptance does not require clean rounds" (F6 drift vs `AGENTS.md`). Theme: the F6/F15/F5 clauses landed in `AGENTS.md` but were not mirrored into `orchestrator.md` (the operational prompt), plus backstop-guard wording drifts across AGENTS.md/orchestrator.md/triager.md. Separate triager (opus, independent of the orchestrator and the implementer) dispatched to adjudicate; it reads BOTH findings files directly (Q-14).

Agent-isolation decision (human, refining the earlier intake): adopt a CAPABILITY-TIERED, harness-agnostic rule. Run each WRITER agent in the strongest isolation the harness supports, preference order: (1) container isolation (agent-box / agent-images) if available, PREFERRED (isolates filesystem + environment + security sandbox); (2) worktree isolation if that is what is available (e.g. claude-code native); (3) discipline-only fallback (scoped fmt, no cross-owner git checkout, commit the ledger before writers) when the harness has no isolation. Read-only agents (reviewers reading, triager) need no isolation (Principle 2). The discipline fix is the always-on BASELINE, not replaced; isolation is the structural upgrade layered on when available. "Container-preferred" is the runtime selection rule (in the workflow docs); our own build/adoption order stays worktree-first (native to claude-code, cheap now), agent-box container integration later; the rule resolves to worktree for us today anyway. Fold into `optional-modules` (isolation) plus a small discipline workflow-rule item at the next planner touch.

Fix = mirror the clauses into `orchestrator.md` + the two README nits. Committing the ledger (protect it), then resuming the implementer WITH the discipline rule (no repo-wide fmt, no git checkout). Review files kept until the step converges, then cleaned up (Q-14).

MISTAKE: I deleted the Q-14 review files with `rm -rf` while they were UNTRACKED (never committed), so their raw content is not recoverable from git; the findings survive only in summary here and in the triager verdicts. Exactly the case the human's new rule prevents.

New human rule (DECIDED): COMMIT BEFORE DELETING any workflow-managed file (findings files, the ledger at task close, any transient artifact), so the deletion is a committed deletion recoverable from git history. Resolves the Q-14 committed-vs-transient sub-decision toward COMMITTED (a findings file is committed at least once, then removed as a committed deletion). Fold into Q-14's cleanup step, the ledger delete-at-close lifecycle, and a general workflow rule. Applies from now.

`pack-rebuild-tracking` implemented (build.rs, std-only, recursive `cargo:rerun-if-changed` for pack/). Validated by the orchestrator: 46 tests pass; a pack edit re-embeds on a plain `just build` with NO `cargo clean` (marker reached the scaffolded output, then reverted; tree clean). The implementer was accidentally killed by the human mid-task but had already reverted its temp edit, so recovery was just: inspect git status (only `?? build.rs`), re-validate.

Robustness-to-mishaps decision (human asked; per the human-input contract): a killed or interrupted agent can leave the tree inconsistent. Adopt LAYERED robustness: (baseline, now) a clean-tree-before-writer invariant (commit pending work before spawning a writer, so a kill's damage is a visible uncommitted diff); an orchestrator RECOVERY PROTOCOL on any kill/interrupt (inspect git status/diff, revert stray temp artifacts, discard or complete partial work, confirm a known-good tree before continuing); and validation-in-scratch (destructive validations run in a temp dir or worktree, not the live tree). (structural, later) ISOLATION (already decided) is the real fix, a killed isolated writer cannot touch the main tree; this is the SECOND mishap (after the fmt/checkout clobber) that is evidence to prioritise it. Unifying theme: git is the workflow's durability/recovery substrate. Fold into the discipline/isolation cluster at the next planner touch.

Robustness-to-mishaps DECIDED by the human: adopt the layered robustness, baseline now (clean-tree-before-writer invariant, orchestrator recovery protocol on any kill/interrupt, validation-in-scratch) and isolation as the structural fix (already decided; this mishap is added evidence to prioritise it). Fold into the discipline/isolation cluster at the next planner touch.

Gate-prompt finding (`Q-20`, from a human question, transcript-recovered). The human asked whether `pack/prompts/clarifying-questions.md` and `open-questions-gate.md` are for humans. A compaction had flattened their real nature (agent-RUN, human-ANSWERED decision GATES: the "me" in them is the human) into "agent-facing role prompts", so the orchestrator first answered wrongly ("not for humans"). Combed the transcript (jq/ripgrep over the 48MB session file): design intent is at [07-11T10:17] ("answer at the gates: clarifying questions before work starts ... you're the decider there") and the same discussion is where the kickoff prompt was born; git shows the files came from `a135ff9`, and `704bbae` made open-questions-gate principle-agnostic after an earlier human worry. Relationship: `kickoff.md` (human-invoked) is the ENTRANCE (human -> workflow); the gates are where the running workflow comes BACK to the human (workflow -> human); `human-onboarding`'s "Getting started" section documents both ends. DECIDED (human): keep the gates as the planner's prompts (do not fold into `planner.md`), fix the "ask me" routing (sub-agent -> orchestrator -> human), and correct the README's "one prompt per role" mislabel. New step `gate-prompt-clarity` inserted before `compaction-prep`. Recording the plan + this finding, then implementing + reviewing.

`gate-prompt-clarity` implemented (commit 41cf5ca; before fc30bb3): reworded both gate prompts to name the human as decider + state sub-agent -> orchestrator -> human routing; fixed the README `prompts/` "one prompt per workflow role" mislabel. LOW-risk -> one clean round.

No high/critical -> no backstop. Round 1 outcome: NEW VALID. Between-step checkpoint finding (`Q-21`, OPEN, from a human question). At a step boundary the orchestrator paused with an ad-hoc "carry on or pause?" that was neither a defined human-input point nor in the human-input-contract format. Two causes: (1) an operator miss, the human-input contract (`Q-12`, still not in the pack until `deliberation-mode` lands) was not self-applied, so the decision was a thin binary instead of options/trade-offs/recommendation/reasoning; (2) a real design gap, the workflow has the orchestrator drive step-to-step autonomously and defines no between-step checkpoint. Recorded `Q-21` (open) for the human to decide the cadence (autonomous / report-only / gate / configurable), recommendation (d)+(b). The FORMAT half is `deliberation-mode`'s job. Human chose to proceed with `deliberation-mode` now; `Q-21` stays open in the queue for the human's decision.

So that finding is dismissed (not a defect); reverted the pointless reflow; recorded the preference in memory (`no-hard-wrap-prose`) and `Q-22`. `no-wrap-convention` implemented (`Q-22`). Part 1 (convention, implementer, commit `4fa5e74`): added a "Prose formatting" paragraph to `pack/AGENTS.md` (prose is not hard-wrapped; line length is never a review finding) and the line-length-never-a-finding rule to `pack/prompts/reviewer.md` and `pack/prompts/triager.md`; added a repo-root `.prettierrc.json` `{ "proseWrap": "never" }`. Part 2 (formatter, orchestrator): a pre-reflow safety fix reworded a ledger line so prettier would not read a literal `>` as a blockquote (`81aa4bd`); then a one-time repo-wide `nix fmt` reflow unwrapped all markdown prose (`dc5f69b`, 29 files, +554/-2530), and the `scaffold-self` recipe now runs `nix fmt` after the raw render so it reaches a stable fixed point. Diagnosed and fixed a treefmt CACHE artifact: files unchanged since before `.prettierrc.json` were stale-cached as clean (from the old `proseWrap: preserve`) and skipped, so a one-time `nix fmt -- --no-cache` was needed to reflow the untouched pack prompts; a fresh checkout has an empty cache, so this is an in-session issue, not a committed defect. Verified: `nix fmt` and `scaffold-self` are idempotent (stable git tree), 46 tests + clippy pass, all markdown ASCII-clean, code fences balanced, the plan Roadmap and ledger tables intact, the plan was already unwrapped (untouched). Classified RISKY / high-blast-radius (repo-wide reflow of shipped pack prompts + a change to the formatter contract and the dogfooding recipe) -> TWO clean rounds required. Round 1: two independent reviewers dispatched (opus reflow-integrity, sonnet convention/tooling-correctness; findings to Q-14 files), separate triager to follow.

`no-wrap-convention` round 1 findings: opus (reflow-integrity) CLEAN, 0 findings (word-diff shows only the three intentional convention additions; structural marker counts identical; generated copies match pack sources). sonnet (convention/tooling) 2 low: F1 = the `scaffold-self` `nix fmt` formats the whole repo, not just generated files (done-but-not-decided, comment misleading); F2 = the justfile behavioural change landed in a `style:` commit. Separate triager (opus, read both files per Q-14, `no-wrap-convention-triage.md`): both VALID low; opus CLEAN verdict independently upheld. F1 fix: KEEP the whole-repo `nix fmt` (scoping would duplicate the manifest dest list against Principle 1), correct the misleading recipe comment, and add a plan note that whole-repo formatting is intentional (treefmt idempotent). F2: ACCEPT the residual risk (do not rewrite unpushed local history; an accepted risk does not block convergence). No high/critical raised or dismissed, so no backstop re-check. Round 1 outcome: NEW VALID FINDINGS (F1); RISKY artifact, streak 0 of 2. Applied the F1 fix (justfile comment + plan note) and recorded F2 acceptance, then round 2 (fresh reviewer); two consecutive clean rounds still required to converge.

Zero findings. ORCHESTRATOR CONVERGENCE CORRECTION: the reviewer asserted this was "the second consecutive clean round" and that convergence was met, but that is wrong and convergence accounting is the orchestrator's, not the reviewer's. Round 1 had a valid finding (F1), so it was NOT clean; round 2 is the FIRST clean round. Streak = 1 of 2 for this RISKY artifact. ROUNDS 4 AND 5 RAN, AND THE PLAN FOLD IS NOW CONVERGED AND MERGED (2026-07-28). LESSON FOR FUTURE LOOPS: run an executability lens EARLY, not at round 4. ACCEPTED RESIDUALS CARRIED FORWARD (the review files are commit-deleted, so these live here now). Accepted because `:34`'s operative closing sentence is PERMISSIVE on the reference and prohibitive only on the enumeration, so `:28` narrows a permission rather than contradicting a requirement, and because `:19` (the operative instruction) forecloses the failure mode independently. The round-5 triager widened the recorded residual beyond what the reviewer described: an implementer trying to satisfy both could write a PARAPHRASED pointing, threading between `:28`'s letter and `:34`'s prohibition while still putting a fresh instance of the `Q-69`-contradicted proposition into the prompt; `:19`'s "do not invent a different rule" bars it, but IF STEP 90 IS BUILT BEFORE `Q-69` IS RESOLVED, its work review must check the checkpoint paragraph against `:28` INCLUDING for a paraphrased pointing. WIDENED by step 92's work-review round 1 (`FN-3`, triaged as a more accurate description of the SAME residual, not a new one, since cause, consequence and cost are unchanged): the trigger is not only REMOVING the row. Module-tagging an existing prompt row, or changing its `dest`, both drop it from the derived set just as silently, and a `dest` change additionally deploys a new file that is itself outside the guarded prefix. The only thing catching any of these today is `manifest::tests::builtin_manifest_lists_the_expected_assets`, an exact-list assertion the guard neither owns nor mentions, so updating that list in the same change makes the orphan silent again. (3) `T-4` and `R2-3` remain accepted from rounds 1 and 3 (a generated-fragment paraphrase in a task-scoped plan record, and an "actor-less" characterisation that is technically false of pre-edit line 71 but whose isolated fix would DESYNCHRONISE this record from the converged step-89 sidecar). `T-5` and `T-7` remain DISMISSED; do not reopen any of these without new evidence.

STEP 93 `checks-runner-worktree-name-collision` IS A NEW DEFERRED STEP (order 93, `low_risk`, needs 1 clean round), branch `plan/test-isolation` (worktree `.claude/worktrees/plan-test-isolation`), UNMERGED. It records a REAL test-suite defect found incidentally by a step-92 triager: `src/checks.rs` names checker worktrees `{pid}-{nanos}`, `nanos()` is a bare `SystemTime::now()` read with no uniqueness guarantee, and cargo runs a crate's tests as THREADS OF ONE PROCESS so the pid is shared, letting two concurrent `run()` calls take over each other's worktree. Observed once (one `cargo test` failed two `checks::tests`, five later runs green); this supplies the mechanism for the previously unexplained flake the ledger recorded during the audit work. Production is SAFE (separate CLI invocations are separate processes); the SUITE is not deterministically green. Round records 217 (r1, 5 valid), 219 (r2, 3 valid), 220 (r3, 2 valid), r4 record NOT YET WRITTEN.

BUT THE ROUND IS NOT CLEAN, AND THE REASON IS NEW INFORMATION RATHER THAN A RE-SEED. ORCHESTRATOR DEFECT (4), recorded like the other three so it is not repeated: THE ORCHESTRATOR ASSERTED A FALSE FACT TO THE HUMAN INSIDE A RECOMMENDATION. It told the human this would be "the project's FIRST waiver ever" and offered "gives W5's integrity join its first test on live data" as a REASON to choose it. Both are false: the plan already carries NINETEEN `[[step.waiver]]` entries, FOUR of them `accepted-at-escalation` + `record-backed`, the exact kind being written. The cause was a bad check, not a bad memory: it grepped the JSONL (which legitimately has zero, that substrate being the legacy form) and the TOML pattern `waiver = \[`, which matches ONLY the empty inline arrays and never the populated `[[step.waiver]]` table syntax. It then reported the empty result as an established fact rather than as a check that might not have matched what it was looking for. THE LESSON: a grep returning zero is evidence about the PATTERN as much as about the repo, and a zero result used as a load-bearing premise must be confirmed by a second pattern that is known to match something. The decision was unaffected (the human's choice is better supported with four precedents than with none) and the correction was given immediately, but the human was briefly reasoning from a false premise the orchestrator supplied.

ALSO WORTH THE SUBSTRATE NOTE: the orchestrator first wrote the waiver as a JSONL `type:"waiver"` record and W3 did NOT clear, because with `[meta].primary = "toml"` the waivers are read by `waivers_from_toml` (`src/workflow.rs:192`) and the JSONL `parse_waivers` path (`:165`) backs only the Markdown-sourced flow. The JSONL record was therefore INERT, and was removed rather than left as a second, unread copy of the same fact (One source of truth). This is the same TOML-supersedes-JSONL shape as `[meta].w4_baseline` versus the `type:"baseline"` record.

CALIBRATION, ANSWERING A HUMAN QUESTION ASKED AT THE CLOSE ("why six rounds, is that normal"): NO, it is an outlier. Across all 77 artifacts ever reviewed in this log the distribution is 1 round x16, 2 x35, 3 x10, 4 x6, 5 x6, 6 x2, 7 x1, 9 x1, so the MEDIAN IS 2 and six puts step 92 joint-third of 77, level with `structured-skeleton` and behind only `driver-output-generation` (7) and `workflow-driver` (9). Those are large structural steps; step 92 is one test file's comment block. Project-wide severity mix for context: 301 low, 76 medium, 12 high, 1 critical. Step 92's distinction is that ALL 15 of its findings were low prose, with zero mechanism defects. THE DIAGNOSED CAUSE: the block's subject is "what does this guard cover", and a coverage claim is unusually easy to falsify at an edge, so each fresh lens found another true-sounding sentence with a false boundary; compounding that, THREE OF THE SIX ROUNDS WERE SELF-INFLICTED, since every fix pass that AUTHORED prose (rounds 1, 2, 4) manufactured the next round's finding while all three deletion-class passes (round 3, and both escalation fixes) re-seeded nothing. This is now the third independent confirmation of that pattern in this task and it should change future practice: CONSTRAIN FIX PASSES TO DELETIONS WHERE THE DEFECT ALLOWS IT, and give the implementer an explicit report-do-not-fix escape valve, which was used correctly on all three of this step's escalation passes.

ROUND 1'S ONE ROUTED DECISION IS TAKEN (2026-08-03, receipt `q_id:"Q-55-ledgerslot"`). `ADV-2`, VALID at `low`: under a divergent pairing `next` prints the REJECTED ledger path as the loop's `ledger:` context slot and, eleven lines later, prints that the same ledger was rejected, while on `--json` the qualifying note is dropped entirely because both note fields are `#[serde(skip)]`, leaving `context.ledger` unqualified beside a `resume_state_absent_reason` the consumer must think to correlate. CHOSEN: ACCEPT AND RECORD IT AS A RESIDUAL, over omitting the `ledger` slot when the ledger is unpairable and over naming what the CHECKED plan would resolve to instead. THE REASONING PRESENTED. It violates no written requirement, since the sidecar requires of an unsafe ledger only that the `RESUME STATE` echo be omitted and never mentions the context slot; the address it prints is ACCURATE, being the path `default_ledger_path` genuinely resolved under the decided source-first rule, so this is a wrong DESTINATION under a divergent pairing rather than borrowed CONTENT, and the human surface prints the contradiction in the same output; and the nearest precedent points the same way, since the sidecar already records `next`'s `review_findings` and `triage_findings` slots as unanchored under exactly this pairing, measured at work review on inc1 and deliberately owned by no increment here (Minimal by default, Ground decisions in evidence). THE OMIT OPTION WAS DECLINED because turning an always-present `ActiveLoop` slot into a sometimes-absent one is a JSON contract change that would want its own reason field by the same argument that produced `Q-55-jsonreason`, which is real scope on a `low` finding. THE RE-ROOT OPTION WAS DECLINED because it would name a ledger path the tool never opened and that may not exist, trading one misleading output for another (Make illegal states unrepresentable). SO `ADV-2` IS NOW A NAMED RESIDUAL BESIDE THE `review_findings` ONE AND MUST NOT BE RE-RAISED WITHOUT NEW EVIDENCE.

ROUND 1 RAN AND IS `new_valid` (2026-08-03, record 263): NINE valid findings after dedup, ONE `high`, FIVE `medium` and THREE `low`, ZERO dismissed, so NO backstop was owed. Streak 0 of 2, round 1 of a cap of 5. Three lenses on mixed models plus a separate triager on a fourth worktree, all four cut from the branch tip so citations resolve against the reviewed text. THE SHAPE OF THE ROUND IS THE OPPOSITE OF THE FIVE BEFORE IT AND THAT IS THE POINT: eight of the nine are TEST-ONLY or DOCUMENTATION findings and exactly ONE is a product defect, where the five plan-review loops on this step produced prose findings almost exclusively. The artifact changed kind, from a specification to the code that implements it, and the finding population changed with it.

THE ADVERSARIAL LENS FOUND THE ONLY PRODUCT DEFECT, WHICH IS THE SECOND CONSECUTIVE LOOP WHERE THE BREAK-IT LENS PAID FOR ITSELF. `ADV-1` (`high`): `next` echoes ANOTHER PROJECT'S `## RESUME STATE` verbatim at exit 0 on inputs where `status --resume` REFUSES THE SAME LEDGER, and on `--json` reports `"resume_state_absent_reason": null`, POSITIVELY ASSERTING the block is genuine. THE CAUSE, which the triager confirmed by independent construction: `checked_plan_root` short-circuits when NO PLAN IS READ, so both of `next`'s containment filters are VACUOUS, while `run_resume` supplies itself a root from the anchors via `resume_roots` exactly as `Q-55-resumepairing` decided. The suite missed it because the one test driving that case uses a TOML-primary source, which always yields a root.

THE ROUND'S DECIDING QUESTION WAS PUT TO THE TRIAGER AND NOT TO THE ORCHESTRATOR, and the answer was not the one either lens had. TWO lenses reproduced a `next` leak of the SAME VISIBLE SHAPE and attributed it to DIFFERENT CAUSES: the adversarial lens to the no-plan-read path, the conformance lens to the IN-ROOT BOUND, which is out of scope by human decision, so that lens filed NOTHING at all. THE TRIAGER BUILT A DISCRIMINATING CONTROL rather than arguing from the code: the same fixture files and the same command, run once with the inner project NESTED inside the outer and once moved to a DISJOINT sibling. Nested leaks both halves; disjoint refuses both. SO THEY ARE TWO DEFECTS, nesting alone accounts for the conformance lens's case, and its non-filing was CORRECT. ADV-1's construction uses two top-level siblings with no nesting anywhere, so nesting cannot be its cause.

THE `Q-55-ledgerdivergence` RESIDUAL IS NOW RULED ON, which discharges the obligation this loop inherited. THE RULING SURVIVES AT THE PRIMITIVE LEVEL: `is_outside_root` and `canonical_project_root` are genuinely shared and never duplicated per surface, so the sidecar's one-predicate claim holds. ADV-1 AMENDS IT RATHER THAN OVERTURNING IT: there is ONE PREDICATE AND TWO ROOT-SUPPLY POLICIES, and the second (`resume_roots`, decided by `Q-55-resumepairing` for "the surface that reads no plan") is INCOMPLETELY APPLIED, because `next` and `status` also read no plan in that configuration and never switch to it. RECORD THE AMENDED FORM, since the original ruling's sentence that the in-root bound is "the one place the two arities disagree" is FALSE: they also disagree on the no-plan-read case, which is disjoint-reachable.

A ZERO-FINDING LENS MISSED A LIVE DEFECT INSIDE ITS OWN REMIT AGAIN, WHICH IS THE SECOND CONSECUTIVE LOOP FOR THIS FAILURE MODE. The conformance lens gathered a real reproduction, attributed it correctly, and then generalised from it to a claim about where the two arities disagree WITHOUT CONSTRUCTING THE DISJOINT CASE that would have falsified the generalisation and handed it ADV-1. The recorded lesson stands and is now doubly evidenced: A CLEAN ROUND IS WORTH WHAT ITS LENSES ACTUALLY EXERCISED, NOT WHAT THEY COLLECTED. THE ORCHESTRATOR'S OWN CONTRIBUTION TO IT IS RECORDED AS DEFECT (13): telling three lenses that the in-root bound was out of scope was right for stopping rediscovery, and it also HANDED A REVIEWER A READY-MADE BUCKET to drop a live-looking reproduction into. The cure is not to drop the scope exclusion, which saved a round, but to REQUIRE A DISCRIMINATING CONTROL whenever a lens attributes something it reproduced to an out-of-scope cause, which is exactly what the triager was told to do and what settled the round.

THE TEST-EVIDENCE LENS RAN 29 MUTATIONS, 22 CAUGHT AND 7 SURVIVED, and its most valuable result is a NEGATIVE one: THE CENTRAL MUTATION IS CAUGHT. Both existing symlink tests symlink a DIRECTORY, never a leaf. REAL RED-THEN-GREEN, COUNTED HONESTLY: 12 of the 13 new integration tests are RED against the pre-fix product, and the thirteenth is a no-regression pin that correctly passes pre-fix and is NOT evidence for this increment. A CORRECTION TO THIS PROJECT'S OWN ASCII CHECK CONVENTION, found by the implementer and confirmed here, which matters because the orchestrator has been running the weaker form: the literal `LC_ALL=C grep -n '[^ -~]'` matches EVERY HARD TAB, since TAB is 0x09 and the class starts at 0x20, so it is unusable on Rust files that this repo indents with tabs and it reports 1507 hits on an untouched `src/workflow.rs` at HEAD. The correct form for tab-indented files is `LC_ALL=C grep -nP '[^\t\x20-\x7e]'`, which reports 0 on all five touched files. The old form remains correct for the Markdown artifacts it was used on, which carry no tabs.

THE DECISION IS TAKEN (2026-08-02, receipt `q_id:"Q-55-endproperty"`), SO THE PARAGRAPH ABOVE IS DISCHARGED AND THE NEXT ACTION IS A PLANNER FOLD. CHOSEN: ROOT THE PREDICATE ON THE PLAN THE CHECK ACTUALLY READS, over layering a second condition on the two roots, and over parsing the whole `(source, plan, metrics)` triple at the boundary into one validated checked-target type. THE ORCHESTRATOR VERIFIED THE MECHANISM IN THE CODE FIRST-HAND rather than relaying the sidecar: `run_validate` resolves the log through `resolve_metrics_path`, which anchors on `--source` first and `--plan` second, while the `--workflow` match arms read a DIFFERENT artifact, the TOML source in the `(Some(source), _, Some(metrics_text))` arm and `--plan` in the `(None, Some(plan_text), Some(metrics_text))` arm. THE REASONING PRESENTED, recorded because it is what makes the choice checkable rather than a preference. (1) It is ONE predicate, not two layered ones, collapsing the whole question to "is the log I am about to read under the root of the plan I am about to check" (Minimal by default). (2) It is a STRICT SUPERSET of the converged anchor-rooted text rather than a replacement: in TOML-primary mode the checked plan IS the anchor, so the rule is unchanged there, and it differs from the converged text ONLY when a `--source` is given and is not TOML-primary, which is exactly the gap. (3) It COVERS THE TYPO'D-`--source` CASE, which the second-condition option does not, because a nonexistent `--source` yields no canonical root and so a two-root comparison has nothing to compare, which is the same no-root clause the sidecar already relies on. (4) The boundary-parse option was rejected on scope, not on merit: it is the cleaner long-term architecture and would make the divergent pairing unconstructible rather than detected (Make illegal states unrepresentable, Prefer the cleaner long-term architecture over the smallest diff), but it touches the resolution shared by `validate`, `status`, `next` and the ledger path, needs its own risk class and probably its own design pass, and risks reopening the already-decided `Q-55-refusalscope` split between the validator refusing and the projections omitting. IF IT IS EVER WANTED it belongs in the validation-constraints step alongside the queued project-identity work, which is the other mechanism that reaches the same class.

THE ONE JUDGEMENT THE PLANNER ESCALATED, AND THE HUMAN'S RULING (2026-08-02). The planner extended the decided rooting to `next`'s DEFAULT LEDGER PATH, a consequence it derived from the code rather than from the receipt's text, and it flagged this in its own report as the one place to push back rather than letting it pass silently, which is the escape valve working as designed. THE ORCHESTRATOR VERIFIED THE DERIVATION FIRST-HAND before putting it to the human: `src/main.rs:default_ledger_path` anchors `source.as_ref().or(plan.as_ref())`, source first, while `run_next` selects its plan TOML-primary-`--source`-else-`--plan`, so a Markdown-primary `--source` in project A paired with a `--plan` in project B makes `next` project B's steps while echoing A's `## RESUME STATE`. That is CONTENT INJECTION INTO AN INSTRUCTION AN AGENT ACTS ON, which this task's design pass already recorded explorer B reproducing, and which the ledger already called arguably the more urgent half. THE HUMAN CHOSE TO KEEP IT IN INC2, over cutting back to `validate` only and filing the ledger case separately, and over building it as its own increment. The reasoning presented: the sidecar's own rule is one predicate never re-implemented per surface, so leaving the ledger rooted on the anchor while the plan root moved would have made the document contradict itself; and splitting it into a separate increment would contradict this step's settled argument, recorded when `Q-55-scope` was decided, that the four call sites share one function and splitting would review the same derivation twice (Minimal by default).

ROUND 1 RAN AND IS `new_valid` (2026-08-02, record 251): SIX valid findings, THREE of them `high`, ZERO dismissed, so NO backstop was owed. Streak 0 of 2, round 1 of a cap of 5. Two lenses on mixed models plus a separate triager on a third worktree, all three from the branch tip. THE EXECUTABILITY LENS AT ROUND 1 PAID FOR ITSELF IMMEDIATELY, which is the whole reason it was moved this early: `EX-1` (high) is that NO ACCEPTANCE CHECK PINS THE DIVERGENT-PAIRING METRICS CASE ON A PROJECTION. Every projection check uses an EXPLICIT `--metrics` outside the anchor's root, which the OLD anchor-rooted predicate catches too, so an implementer could root the ledger predicate on the checked plan, leave the metrics predicate on the anchor, PASS THE ENTIRE CHECK SET, and still have `next` print `state: converged` and `next: mark the step complete` for a foreign project. The reviewer reproduced the fabricated instruction and the RESUME STATE echo for real at exit 0. A check that passes before the change is worthless, and five of this file's projection checks were that.

THE ORCHESTRATOR'S OWN REASONING WAS FALSIFIED BY `EX-3`, AND THE CORRECTION IS RECORDED HERE RATHER THAN QUIETLY DROPPED. Reasoning point (2) in the paragraph above, that the new rooting is a STRICT SUPERSET of the converged anchor-rooted text differing ONLY on the gap case, IS FALSE, and it was presented to the human as a ground for choosing this mechanism. The triager constructed both directions and MEASURED them: the refusal sets are INCOMPARABLE, NOT NESTED. Direction 1, `--source fixC/docs/plans/x.plan.toml --plan fixC/notes/p.md` greens today and the checked plan's root resolves to `.../fixC/notes` through `src/main.rs:project_root_of_source`'s fallback to the plan's own parent when there is no `docs/plans`-shaped ancestor, so THE NEW RULE REFUSES A CORRECT SAME-PROJECT RUN. Direction 2, `--source fixA/... --plan fixB/... --metrics fixB/own-log` is B-against-B and the new rule ALLOWS it where an anchor rooting refuses it. The clause "which is exactly the gap" is false too: the gap is one of at least THREE distinguishable cases in that region. WHAT SURVIVES is only the narrower region claim, that the two rootings coincide in TOML-primary mode and where no `--source` is given. Grounds (1) and (3) are unaffected and the triager reproduced the typo'd-`--source` case that carries (3). A RE-DECISION WAS NOT INDICATED and was not taken: the REJECTED second-condition alternative does not avoid direction 1 either, because for that fixture the anchor root `.../fixC` and the checked root `.../fixC/notes` differ so a two-root condition fires on the same run, and the layout is ALREADY refused today in its no-`--source` spelling, so the amendment removes a rescue rather than introducing a new species.

TWO FURTHER HUMAN DECISIONS CLOSED ROUND 1'S OPEN QUESTIONS (2026-08-02), both put with the correction above stated first. `Q-55-conventionlesscost` (receipt): ACCEPT DIRECTION 1 AND RECORD IT AS A THIRD ACCEPTED COST beside the existing (i) silent-miss-inside-the-right-project and (ii) symlinked-`docs/plans` costs, and PIN IT WITH A CHECK, over carving the conventionless case out of the predicate and over reopening the boundary-parse option. Carving it out was declined because it would REVERSE `Q-55-noconvention`, which decided the containment refusal is layered ON TOP of the source's-own-directory fallback rather than suppressed by it. `Q-55-resumepairing` (receipt): CLOSE THE `status --resume` LEAK IN INC2, over filing it to the validation-constraints step and over reopening the boundary parse. THE DEFECT, which the triager caught as the MIRROR of a question already asked and answered for `next` but never asked for this surface: `status --resume --source A --plan B` prints project A's `## RESUME STATE` on the DEFAULT ledger path at exit 0 and SURVIVES INC2 AS SPECIFIED, because that surface reads no plan and so keeps the anchor rooting by design, while the sidecar separately asserts the default ledger case "is already closed by the anchoring in inc1". THE FRAMING THAT KEEPS THE ONE-PREDICATE RULE INTACT, and the reason this is not a per-surface re-implementation: the rule stays "root on the plan the check reads", and where a surface reads NO plan the two NAMED plans must agree or the block is omitted, which SUPPLIES a root where there is no checked plan rather than inventing a second rule.

`R2A-2` (high) IS THE SAME DEFECT SPECIES AS ROUND 1's `EX-2`, REAPPEARING INSIDE THE FIX FOR `EX-3`: accepted cost (iii) and its new pinning check 19b both omit the load-bearing precondition that the `--source` must be MARKDOWN-PRIMARY. CORRECTION, APPENDED RATHER THAN REWRITTEN PER THIS TASK'S OWN CONVENTION: THE BOUND RECORDED ABOVE FOR `Q-55-conventionlesscost` IS FALSE AS WRITTEN. The bound is true only under TWO QUALIFIERS THAT WERE DROPPED IN TRANSCRIPTION: it needs an explicit `--metrics` naming the real log, and it is a claim about a POST-INC2 build rather than the present tense "ALREADY". THE LESSON, which is the third instance of one species in this task: a bound MEASURED with a qualifier loses the qualifier when it is transcribed into a summary, exactly as the aggregate-restating-a-detail defects the inc1 loop kept finding.

A NEW ORCHESTRATOR DEFECT, (12), CAUSED BY THE ORCHESTRATOR AND CAUGHT BY THE PLANNER'S ESCAPE VALVE. THE DEFECT: the orchestrator appended the `Q-55-resumecost` receipt to `docs/metrics/workflow.jsonl` DURING an open review loop, and the artifact under review ASSERTED A COUNT OF THOSE RECEIPTS ("NINE decision receipts"), so an orchestrator integration edit SILENTLY FALSIFIED A CLAIM IN THE ARTIFACT ITS OWN REVIEWERS HAD JUST VERIFIED AS TRUE. The round-2 triage had checked NINE and found it correct; the tenth receipt landed after. NO REVIEWER OR TRIAGER COULD HAVE CAUGHT THIS, because the falsification happened after their reads, which is what makes it a distinct defect rather than a missed finding. THE PLANNER FOUND IT during the fix pass, RAISED IT WITHOUT FIXING IT because no finding named that line, and NAMED THE MINIMAL REMEDY ITSELF. THE CURE, APPLIED: the orchestrator sent the producer back for a SINGLE AUTHORISED ONE-WORD DELETION, removing the asserted count entirely rather than updating it to TEN, so it cannot go stale again; this is the same deletion-class remedy the triage prescribed for `R2A-7` and for `R2A-4`'s "TWO CASES REACH IT", and the planner confirmed zero words authored and no other count moved by a full uppercase-number-word census. THE STANDING RULE THIS ESTABLISHES: an artifact under review MUST NOT assert a count of anything the orchestrator appends to during the loop (receipts, round records, escalations), because the orchestrator's own integration edits are then a source of findings that no review pass can prevent. Prefer no count at all over a maintained one.

`R3B-1` WAS DISMISSED, AND THE DISMISSAL IS MORE INSTRUCTIVE THAN THE FINDING. The cold reader claimed the acceptance-check set never re-runs the no-regression correct case after inc2, so nothing pins the lexical/canonical split the document says MUST NOT BE COLLAPSED. The triager verified all three legs: legs 1 and 2 REPRODUCED (check 9 is labelled AFTER INC1, and nothing in the inc2 block re-runs it), but LEG 3 IS FALSE. The same test file the reviewer cited contains `the_correct_case_prints_the_same_relative_paths_it_always_did`, whose doc comment opens "Acceptance check 9, the Safe on existing projects pin", runs a BARE RELATIVE `--source` from the project root via `.current_dir(dir)`, and `assert_eq!`s the WHOLE stdout against the three relative spellings inline. The triager measured that the printed paths are the resolved paths verbatim, so a collapsed split FAILS that assertion, making the suite pin STRICTER than the check the reviewer said was missing. The reviewer had read the cross-project half of the file, which the module doc itself describes as content-asserted, and generalised to the whole file. Dismissed outright rather than accepted as residual. NO backstop was owed: the finding was `medium`, and the backstop covers only dismissed `high` or `critical`.

THE ONE VALID FINDING WAS DEFECT (12) AGAIN, AND THE TRIAGER OVERRULED THE ORCHESTRATOR'S EARLIER REMEDY. Both round-3 lenses independently found that the PROVENANCE list had NINE bullets for TEN receipts, the missing one being `Q-55-resumecost`. The orchestrator had already been offered this choice once, when the planner raised the stale numeral, and had taken the cheaper half: delete the word "NINE". THAT WAS INSUFFICIENT, and the triager said so plainly: a nine-bullet list under "with decision receipts in `docs/metrics/workflow.jsonl` ... all carrying `task:...`" ASSERTS ITS COUNT IN UNARY, so removing the numeral satisfied the standing rule's letter and missed its subject. IT ALSO REJECTED ADDING THE TENTH BULLET, which is what both reviewers prescribed, on evidence from this very loop: FOUR of the ten receipts landed DURING this review loop and one landed AFTER the round-2 triage had verified the then-current registry, so a tenth bullet restores sync only until the next human decision and round 4 or 5 finds the identical finding with an eleventh. WHAT SURVIVES CONTINUED APPENDS IS THE SELECTOR, NOT A SNAPSHOT: the file path plus the `task:` filter stays correct however many receipts land (One source of truth, which the document invokes by name at two places). Before prescribing deletion it VERIFIED that all ten receipts are separately cited in the body at the section each governs, eight with human-and-date attribution, so the registry carried no information of its own. It also CORRECTED a sub-claim inside the valid finding: "the last taken on 2026-08-02" asserts a DATE, not an identity, and is true today.

THE ROUND 3 FIX PASS IS DONE (commit `dd54227`) AND IT IS THE BEST-CONDITIONED PASS OF THE THREE: A PURE DELETION WITH ZERO AUTHORED WORDS, 1 insertion and 11 deletions per file, the sidecar and the projection identical so nothing is hand-made, ledger and review files untouched, zero line citations. The planner verified BEFORE deleting that each of the ten receipt ids still appears in the document body outside the deleted passage, and reported all ten with line numbers; `Q-55-resumecost` keeps its single body citation at the accepted cost (iv) record, which is the attribution the triage relied on. It also reported, unprompted, that the log GREW UNDER THE FILE while the pass ran, from 255 records to 256, which is defect (12)'s mechanism observed live and is the strongest possible confirmation that deletion rather than a tenth bullet was the right call.

THE TRIAGER'S CONVERGENCE JUDGEMENT, WHICH THE ORCHESTRATOR ACCEPTED AND ACTED ON: DO NOT ESCALATE, RUN ROUNDS 4 AND 5. Its ground: the mechanism, increment split, risk classifications, accepted-cost set and check structure have survived THREE ROUNDS AND SIX INDEPENDENT LENSES with zero falsified claims; every finding across all three rounds was in freshly authored prose; and round 3's single finding was not even that, being a bookkeeping copy going out of sync with a log the orchestrator itself appends to, an artefact of the loop being OPEN rather than a defect in the plan. Two clean rounds is realistic because a zero-authored-word pass leaves nothing for round 4 to catch that round 3 created, and this project now has FOUR independent confirmations that deletion-class passes re-seed nothing. ITS STATED TRIPWIRE, which the orchestrator will honour: ESCALATE IF ROUND 4 RETURNS ANYTHING ABOVE `low`, OR ANY FINDING IN THE MECHANISM RATHER THAN THE PROSE.

ROUND 4 IS OPEN WITH THE LENS THIS LOOP HAS BEEN BUILDING TOWARD. Since the prose seam is mined out and six lenses found ZERO mechanism defects, round 4's primary lens is ADVERSARIAL ATTACK ON THE MECHANISM (opus): its goal is to BREAK inc2 as specified by CONSTRUCTING a false green or an unrecorded false refusal, building fixtures and running them rather than reading, and it is explicitly forbidden from reporting prose, wording, counts or citations. It must keep an ATTACK LOG including the attacks that FAILED, because on a clean result the failed attacks are what makes "no defect found" mean anything. The second lens is DELETION VERIFICATION plus a final whole-document consistency sweep (sonnet). Models swapped again so no lens repeats a model-and-role pairing.

ROUND 4 RAN, THE ADVERSARIAL LENS BROKE THE MECHANISM, AND THE BACKSTOP FIRED FOR THE FIRST TIME IN THIS TASK AND OVERTURNED A DISMISSAL (record 257, outcome `new_valid`, 3 valid: 1 high, 1 medium, 1 low; `dismissal_recheck` record 258 with `result` `overturned`). The verification lens returned ZERO findings, so every round-4 finding came from the adversarial lens. Streak stays 0 of 2 at round 4 of a cap of 5, so EVEN A PERFECTLY CLEAN ROUND 5 REACHES ONLY 1 and CONVERGENCE WITHIN THE CAP IS IMPOSSIBLE; escalation was certain and spending round 5 would have bought nothing.

THE ADVERSARIAL LENS EARNED THE ROUND. Sixteen valid findings across rounds 1 to 3, EVERY ONE IN PROSE, and six lenses had found ZERO mechanism defects; that is precisely why round 4's primary lens was told to BREAK the mechanism by construction rather than read it, and forbidden from reporting prose, counts or citations. IT SUCCEEDED: `R4A-1`, THE CONTAINMENT PREDICATE IS VACUOUS FOR ANY PROJECT NESTED INSIDE THE CHECKED PLAN'S ROOT. A nested project's log lies UNDER that root, so the predicate is silent, `validate --workflow` prints `workflow invariants hold` at exit 0 for a step with no evidence of its own, and `next` prints `state: converged` and `mark the step complete`. CONTAINMENT REFUSES ONLY WHAT LIES OUTSIDE THE SUBTREE. It ran 24 attacks and logged the 21 that FAILED, which is what makes the three that landed mean something; the most dangerous non-break was a `--metrics` escaping the root through a NONEXISTENT intermediate component, which would have defeated the guard by pure prefix match and fails only because the kernel will not open such a path, so the specified resolution is safe there BY AN OS PROPERTY RATHER THAN BY CONSTRUCTION.

THE TRIAGER DISMISSED THE HIGH AND WAS HALF RIGHT, WHICH IS WHY THE BACKSTOP EXISTS. It matched `R4A-1` to round 1's `EX-5`, already ruled valid at `low` and fixed by a deletion that landed, and it CORRECTED THE REVIEWER'S STATED CAUSE by building a fixture with both projects conventionally laid out and NO fallback anywhere, getting the identical false green and proving `Q-55-noconvention`'s fallback is IRRELEVANT to it. Both of those hold and the backstop confirmed both independently. BUT ITS SCOPE SWEEP NAMED THREE SITES AND MISSED THE LEDGER HALF. THE BACKSTOP OVERTURNED THE DISMISSAL on two claims that are ADDED BY THIS FOLD and that the false green falsifies: a WHOLLY INSERTED sentence claiming "The predicate rooted on the checked plan catches that; an anchor-rooted one cannot", against which its fixture had `next` echo a marker line from project A's ledger under the outer project's plan, uncaught; and the `ledger-not-this-project` vocabulary definition, stated as a PROJECT-IDENTITY condition with no containment qualifier, contradicting its own leading clause and matching a second condition the same fold explicitly REJECTS elsewhere. Neither was ruled by any round, verified by grepping all eleven prior review files. The backstop reached its verdict having tried SIX distinct routes to overturn and reporting each that failed, which is the posture it was briefed into: try to overturn, and let the failures make a surviving dismissal safe.

THE BACKSTOP CORRECTED BOTH EARLIER AGENTS ON OWNERSHIP, AND THIS IS THE MOST CONSEQUENTIAL THING IT FOUND. The reviewer assigned the defect to the FALLBACK root cause queued to the validation-constraints step; the triager assigned it to the PROJECT-IDENTITY work queued to the same step. IT BELONGS TO NEITHER: project identity is a filter over `Round` records and `[meta]`, and IT CANNOT CHANGE WHICH LEDGER FILE `run_next` OPENS. So the ledger half of this false green currently has NO OWNER anywhere in the plan. FOUR INDEPENDENT READERS HAVE NOW REDISCOVERED THIS BOUND FROM SCRATCH and the two most recent each got its cause wrong in a DIFFERENT way, which the backstop diagnosed: the document records the bound through a SINGLE INSTANCE, the copied log, whose mechanism is a COPY while the actual rule is a SUBTREE, so readers keep reaching for the wrong cause. Its judgement, labelled as a judgement and separate from its verdict: RECORDING ONE INSTANCE RATHER THAN THE RULE IS NOT ADEQUATE.

ESCALATION DECIDED (2026-08-03, escalation record 259, `human_decision` `decision`): FIX THE FILED FINDINGS, STATE THE RULE, RUN ONE VERIFICATION ROUND, THEN MERGE. Chosen over fixing only what is filed and merging, over changing the mechanism inside inc2, and over authoring a new step for the subtree gap. WHICH BRANCH OF THE COUNTER RULE APPLIES, STATED EXPLICITLY BECAUSE THE TWO BRANCHES GIVE DIFFERENT BARS: this is the "send it back for a specific fix that closes this loop" branch, so THE COUNTERS RETIRE with the decision rather than reset. The RESET branch would be wrong here and would SILENTLY RAISE THE BAR, exactly as step 92's escalation record spells out: this artifact is `risky` at 2 consecutive clean rounds, so a reset plus the one authorised round would end at streak 1 of 2 and converge NOTHING. Under the retire branch the verification round is a CHECK ON THAT SPECIFIC FIX, did it land as prescribed and did it re-seed anything, and a clean result MERGES.

THE `Q-55-endproperty` FOLD IS MERGED (2026-08-03, ff-merged to main at `d11e4ef`). POST-MERGE ALL GREEN: 262 records valid, 95 steps and 69 questions valid, workflow invariants hold, `render --check` up to date, 395 tests passing across eight binaries with 0 failures, clippy silent under `-D warnings`, tree clean, NO worktrees. FINAL SHAPE against main: the sidecar 39 added / 32 removed, the plan TOML 1/1, and the generated projection 40/33, which is exactly the sidecar plus the TOML and therefore carries nothing hand-made.

THE VERIFICATION ROUND WAS NOT CLEAN (record 260, 3 valid: 2 medium, 1 low, plus 1 accepted residual, nothing dismissed, no backstop owed), so the escalation's authorised path of fix-one-round-merge ran out and the artifact went back to the human. `Q-55-verifyclose` DECIDED: APPLY ALL THREE AND MERGE WITH NO FURTHER ROUND, over applying then running one more round, and over fixing only the two mediums. The final pass is `d11e4ef`: 19 authored against 35 deleted, NET MINUS 16, three lines per file, two of the three fixes pure or near-pure deletion, and the one that authors most LIFTS 7 OF ITS 10 WORDS from the vocabulary entry both round-5 lenses had ruled true.

A ZERO-FINDING LENS IS NOT EVIDENCE OF CORRECTNESS UNLESS IT APPLIED THE EVIDENCE IT GATHERED, AND THIS LOOP PROVED IT. The two verification-round lenses reached OPPOSITE conclusions about the same sentence. The truth lens BUILT the reversed-nesting fixture, MEASURED it correctly, and even WROTE that reversing which project contains which flips the verdict, then applied that fixture only to a different claim and tested the sentence on sibling and forward-nested pairs only. IT NEVER CROSSED ITS OWN FALSIFIER WITH THE SENTENCE, and returned zero findings. The triager built both directions itself and ruled for the verification lens. Carry this: a clean round is worth what its lenses actually exercised, not what they collected.

THE MEASURED FIX-PASS RULE IS NOW SHARPER, AND THE OLD STATEMENT OF IT WAS WRONG IN A WAY THAT MATTERED. This ledger has said for several loops that AUTHORED PROSE re-seeds while DELETION does not. The round-5 triager measured the escalation fix and refined it: TWO of the four unpriced sites were CLEAN and ruled TRUE by both lenses, while the three that failed shared ONE property, EACH STATED A SCOPE OR OUTCOME MORE GENERALLY THAN WHAT WAS MEASURED. The symmetric nesting qualifier generalised a DIRECTIONAL behaviour; the fallback-direction clause generalised the backstop's claim about costs (iii) and (iv) into a claim about the fallback while dropping that backstop's own "sufficient and not necessary" result; the ledger-echo clause stated on both surfaces what is true of one. SO THE OPERATIONAL RULE IS NOT "AUTHOR FEWER WORDS", IT IS: STATE CLAIMS IN THE DIRECTION THEY WERE MEASURED, IN VOCABULARY THE DOCUMENT ALREADY DEFINES. Word count is a proxy for that, and a poor one; the winning fix here authored 19 words and was safe because 7 of them were lifted rather than coined.

TWO RESIDUALS ARE CARRIED PAST THE MERGE. DO NOT RE-RAISE EITHER WITHOUT NEW EVIDENCE, AND DO NOT LET THE SECOND VANISH. (1) `R5A-4` (low, ACCEPTED): the escalation fix's prescribed edit at accepted cost (ii) orphaned a second antecedent, "and the guard wins", where the planner repaired only the first; accepted because the clause is NOT false and the same sentence supplies the contrast as a measurement. (2) THE TWO-SURFACE LEDGER DIVERGENCE, `Q-55-ledgerdivergence` DECIDED: RECORD IT AS A NAMED RESIDUAL NOW, over folding it into inc2's own work review and over giving it a backlog step. THE ITEM: the `status --resume` rule is a SAME-ROOT AGREEMENT test between the two named plans, while the `next` ledger rule is a CONTAINMENT test on the checked plan's root, and the sidecar separately claims the predicate is NEVER RE-IMPLEMENTED PER SURFACE. Those are two different tests on one concept. THE ROUND-4 BACKSTOP raised it as adjacent, the round-5 verification lens flagged that it SURVIVES the fix, and NO ROUND HAS RULED ON IT. It is recorded here rather than in the plan because it is a question about a mechanism not yet built; WHOEVER REVIEWS INC2's IMPLEMENTATION MUST RULE ON IT, and it is the first thing to test once both surfaces exist in code and the divergence is measurable rather than argued.

SIX-ROUND SCORECARD FOR CALIBRATION: 22 valid findings across five review rounds plus a verification round (r1 6, r2 9, r3 1, r4 3, verification 3), severities falling 3-high / 2-high / none / 1-high / none, ONE dismissal, ONE BACKSTOP FIRING WHICH OVERTURNED THAT DISMISSAL, and one escalation. THE BACKSTOP'S FIRST FIRING IN THIS TASK'S HISTORY EARNED ITS PLACE IMMEDIATELY: it overturned a well-argued dismissal by finding two claims ADDED BY THE FOLD that the triager's scope sweep had missed, having tried SIX distinct routes to overturn and reported each failure. THE ADVERSARIAL LENS WAS THE OTHER DECISIVE CHOICE: sixteen valid findings across rounds 1 to 3 were ALL PROSE and six lenses found ZERO mechanism defects, and only the lens explicitly told to BREAK the thing rather than read it found the in-root false green. LESSON FOR FUTURE LOOPS, alongside the existing run-executability-early one: WHEN SEVERAL ROUNDS RETURN ONLY PROSE FINDINGS, THAT IS EVIDENCE THE PROSE SEAM IS MINED OUT, NOT EVIDENCE THE ARTIFACT IS SOUND; SWITCH A LENS TO ADVERSARIAL CONSTRUCTION RATHER THAN RUNNING A SEVENTH READER.

THE RECEIPT'S OWN PRECEDENT, checked rather than assumed: five `Q-55-<name>` sub-decision receipts already exist on this step (`Q-55-scope`, `Q-55-mechanism`, `Q-55-noconvention`, `Q-55-refusalscope`, `Q-55-jsonreason`), so a mechanism sub-decision under an already-decided question gets a receipt here even though no new `[[question]]` is registered and W4 requires none. NEW EVIDENCE FOR `Q-69`, recorded where the question can find it: WRITING A DECISION RECEIPT IS NOT IN THE `ISOLATION_POLICY_FRAGMENT`'S ENUMERATION EITHER. The fragment (`src/isolation_policy.rs:ISOLATION_POLICY_FRAGMENT`, rendered to `AGENTS.md:91`) says "The only edits made directly on main are ... : flipping a step's status, declaring an increment, recording a round record, and moving the ledger's resume anchor", four items, and a `type:"decision"` record is none of them. It plainly satisfies the enumeration's OWN governing criterion, being an integration-level record that authors no reviewed product content, exactly like the round record beside it, and this project's history has the orchestrator writing them on main throughout. So this is a SIXTH candidate item for the same exhaustiveness problem `Q-69` already carries from `DEC-1`, and it is the second instance found by simply doing the work rather than by reading the sentence. The receipt was written directly on main on that reasoning; spawning an isolated writer to append one JSONL line would be disproportionate (Minimal by default), and the alternative of not recording the decision at all would lose the auditable evidence the human-input contract was met.

THE ESCAPE VALVE WAS NOT USED, AND THE DEVIATION RAN THE OTHER WAY, which is the more useful result of this pass. THE TRIAGER'S PRESCRIPTION FOR `INC2-6` WAS ITSELF UNDER-SCOPED and the planner caught it instead of applying it literally. Confirmed first-hand by the orchestrator at `docs/plans/agent-scaffold.steps/workflow-enforcement-tier.md:198-203`: line 198's numeral HEADS the bullet list at 200-203, so the prescribed "Three doc claims" to "Four doc claims" edit WITHOUT a fourth bullet would have left the count contradicting its own list, and the triager's own twin grep (`'Three doc claims'`, count 1) never reached the numeral twin at 354 ("THE THREE DOC COMMENTS ... all three"). The planner took FOUR sites rather than two, and its one authored bullet at 203 is the necessary member. THE LESSON, which belongs in future fix-pass briefs beside the escape valve: A TRIAGER'S SITE COUNT IS A MEASUREMENT, NOT AN INSTRUCTION, and a producer who finds it short should report and widen rather than apply a prescription that manufactures a contradiction. The escape valve covers the case where the prescribed fix is too big or wrong; this is its mirror, where the prescribed fix is too small, and both are successes.

THREE THINGS THE PLANNER NOTICED AND DELIBERATELY DID NOT FIX under the fix-nothing-adjacent constraint, recorded here so round 3 inherits them rather than losing them. (i) Check 11's before-state is now stated against a fixture whose single step is `not-started`, where W3 passes VACUOUSLY, so that check's red is weaker than 14b's. (ii) Line 205's `A FIFTH ITEM` paragraph and the new bullet at 203 describe enumerations short by one in ADJACENT paragraphs, which a fresh reviewer may reasonably read as overlapping. ORCHESTRATOR DEFECT (10), A NEAR-REPEAT OF (8), CAUGHT WITHIN ONE CALL BOTH TIMES. Twice this session the orchestrator wrote a `cd` into a worktree at the head of a command whose real work used `git -C` or needed no working directory at all, and both times that `cd` PERSISTED into the following call. Nothing landed on the wrong branch, because both were caught immediately and the shell was returned to the repo root before anything else ran, but this is the identical mechanism that once put commit `7400f12` and a decision receipt on the branch instead of main and made a `render --check` the orchestrator REPORTED TO THE HUMAN vacuous. THE RECORDED CURE, "never let a `cd` into a worktree persist", IS TOO WEAK, because it asks the orchestrator to remember to undo something after the fact. THE STRENGTHENED CURE, which replaces it: never type the `cd` at all. Use `git -C <path>`, absolute paths, or a subshell `(cd <path> && ...)` whose scope ends with the command. A bare `cd` into a worktree is a defect ON SIGHT, whether or not a correction follows it.

TWO CHANGES TO THE ROUND 3 BRIEFS, BOTH EARNED BY MEASURED FAILURES AND NEITHER OPTIONAL. (1) THE TENSE RULE, which is the most transferable lesson this review has produced: BOTH demonstrated coverage misses in this review happened because a reviewer tested a claim about a FUTURE state the way you test a claim about the CURRENT tree, opened the cited code, confirmed the sentence is true of the tree, and passed it. A PLAN IS A SET OF CLAIMS ABOUT WHAT WILL EXIST, so that habit systematically passes exactly the sentences a plan exists to get right. Brief every plan-review lens to evaluate each claim AGAINST WHAT THE INCREMENT PRODUCES, not against the tree, and to say which tense it applied when the two differ. (2) ASSIGN FINDING-ID PREFIXES UP FRONT, per reviewer, because both round-2 reviewers independently used `R2-1` for different findings and the triager had to rename them; that is orchestrator defect (9), assigning distinct FILENAMES but not distinct ID NAMESPACES. ALSO KEEP, having earned their place: the enumeration discipline (reviewers LIST what they swept rather than asserting thoroughness, which is what made the round-2 miss visible and attributable), the explicit permission that a clean result is a good outcome and findings must not be manufactured, and reviewer MODEL DIVERSITY.

THE DELIBERATE COVERAGE CORRECTION WAS RIGHT AND SHOULD BE REPEATED. Inc1 and inc3 had never had a dedicated deep read (round 1 read the whole sidecar as instructions and as fidelity; round 2's deep lens read inc2 only), and the fresh lens produced 7 of the 8 findings. FOUR of its seven are the same shape: A DEFECT AN EARLIER ROUND ESTABLISHED, SURVIVING AT A TWIN SITE THE EARLIER FIX'S GREP COULD NOT MATCH. `R3B-1` is the clearest and is the most important finding of the round: the ordering argument's premise at `:290`, that inc3 is "the only increment that makes a previously-green run fail", is falsified TWICE INSIDE THE SAME DOCUMENT, by inc2's own risk paragraph at `:298` and by inc1's acceptance check, and it is round 1's `EX-5` at a site the `EX-5` fix could not reach. That is now the third time this fold has been bitten by a twin a literal string grep cannot match, which is the standing argument for the semantic-twin sweep added to the triage brief.

THE TENSE RULE PAID FOR ITSELF ON ITS FIRST OUTING, which settles whether to keep it. `R3B-3`: check 1 pins `cargo test` at "386 expected", THE PRE-CHANGE COUNT, while `:304` requires each increment to add tests, so the check AS WRITTEN PASSES an implementation that adds no test and FAILS a correct one. That sentence is TRUE OF TODAY'S TREE and FALSE OF THE TREE THE INCREMENT PRODUCES, which is precisely the class the rule was written to catch and precisely the class three prior rounds walked past while confirming the count was current. `R3B-5` is the same rule applied to check 15, whose "reports the missing log BY PATH" is already true of the pre-fix build, so only the exit code discriminates and the literal minimal implementation ships a problem reading "skipping the workflow check" on a run that exits 1 while passing every check. THE ID-PREFIX FIX ALSO WORKED: `R3A-` and `R3B-` collided nowhere and the triager renamed nothing, against round 2 where both reviewers used `R2-1`. Keep assigning prefixes per reviewer.

THE TRIAGER DECLINED BOTH AVAILABLE SHORTCUTS, IN OPPOSITE DIRECTIONS. It ruled all eight VALID with zero accept-residual and zero dismissals, AND it considered `medium` for `R3B-1` and `R3B-5` and HELD `low` on both, recording that severity is absolute impact if unfixed and that lineage from an earlier `medium` "is no more a merit than the round a finding arrives in". That is the step-92 round-4 triager's formula applied in the OPPOSITE direction, against inflation rather than against dismissal, and it is the second time this project's triage layer has stated the round-number-is-not-a-merit rule unprompted. It also RULED AGAINST MERGING `R3A-1` with `R3B-5` despite their shared theme (an acceptance check clause satisfied on both sides of the change, round 1's `EX-8` genus), distinguishing them by which half of the check is weak, by what the fix edits, and by root cause.

CITATIONS CORRECTED AND FACTS ADDED BY THE TRIAGER, the `Q-66` grounding rule earning its keep for the fourth consecutive loop. `R3A-1`'s `src/workflow.rs:437` does NOT support the claim attached to it, and NO code citation could, since the predicate in question is inc2's UNBUILT work; the finding survives with that citation struck and its true grounds at `:164` and `:180`. THE ROUND 3 FIX PASS IS THE BEST-CONDITIONED OF THE THREE, on the measure this project has calibration data for. ARITHMETIC AFTER ROUND 3, stated plainly because it now constrains everything: STREAK 0 OF 2, with rounds 4 and 5 left against a cap of 5, so CONVERGENCE INSIDE THE CAP REQUIRES BOTH REMAINING ROUNDS TO COME BACK CLEAN.
