STEP 93'S ESCALATION IS DECIDED AND THE HOLD IS LIFTED (human, 2026-07-31, escalation record 232, `human_decision` `decision`). THIS SUPERSEDES THE HOLD PARAGRAPH BELOW. THE DECISION: apply ONLY THE TWO PURE DELETIONS and ACCEPT `RG4`'s narrowing clause AS A RESIDUAL, then ONE VERIFICATION ROUND, then merge with a waiver. WHICH BRANCH OF THE COUNTER RULE APPLIES, stated explicitly because the two branches give different bars and step 92's record shows the trap: this is the "send it back for a specific fix that closes this loop" branch, so the counters RETIRE rather than reset. The RESET branch would be strictly worse here, as the analyst independently confirmed: resetting plus one authorised round ends at streak 1 of 2 and converges NOTHING.

WHY THE FIX SET WAS REDUCED, AND IT IS AN EVIDENCE DECISION RATHER THAN A SHORTCUT. The analyst stated a conditional against its own primary recommendation: at the full three-fix set its confidence in one verification round is MEDIUM, but "if step 93's fix set were cut to the two pure deletions only, I would raise that to HIGH". The ground is that `RG4`'s narrowing clause is THE FIX SET'S ONLY AUTHORED PROSE, and 20 of the 22 recorded injections corpus-wide are `class = prose` (90.9 percent, 72.2 to 97.5). `RG4` IS THEREFORE AN ACCEPTED RESIDUAL, DO NOT RE-RAISE IT: it is a `low` over-claim in a comment stating that widening the prune by prefix would close a window it cannot reach for an orphan recorded under a different `TMPDIR`, the sentence it sits in uniquely carries the Principle 18 least-authority reason the module declines to widen the prune, and the routed-out temp-dir step inherits that sentence anyway. `AD1b`, `X1`, `X5`, `X6`, `X7` and `RG3` remain accepted residuals from earlier rounds on the same terms.

THE VERIFICATION ROUND'S STANDING, so nobody mistakes it for a fresh loop: it is a CHECK ON THAT SPECIFIC REDUCED FIX, asking only whether the two deletions landed as authorised and whether anything was re-seeded, NOT the first round of a new convergence streak. A clean result MERGES. The convergence shortfall is then waived exactly as step 92's was, `unit` `increment`, `reason` `accepted-at-escalation`, `evidence_tier` `record-backed`, `evidence` joining to escalation record 232.

FORWARD INSTRUMENTATION IS SPLIT, CHEAP-DETERMINISTIC NOW AND DESIGN-REQUIRING LATER (human, 2026-07-31). SCHEDULED NOW as mechanical wins that need no design pass and that catch defects already PROVEN to exist: (a) split `valid_findings` from a new `fix_required_findings`, since the field demonstrably carries two meanings at a 15.7 percent inconsistency rate; (b) add `len(severities) == valid_findings` and `sum(reviewers[].valid_findings) >= valid_findings` as DETERMINISTIC validator checks, which would have caught all 22 of the per-reviewer mismatches including the 3 that are inexplicably higher; (c) ENFORCE A PHASE SLOT in findings-file names, since the shipped `<step>-<role>-<disambiguator>` convention has none and two review loops on one step slug have already collided in a way that corrupted a round-to-file join. DEFERRED TO A PLANNER AS ONE STEP, because each needs design: the FOUR-VALUE provenance vocabulary with a scope qualifier and made REQUIRED rather than left a triager habit; recording `fix_pass_commits` and `fix_pass_branch` on the round record, since writer isolation destroys the timeline archaeology needs and left 72 percent of pairs unresolvable; measuring fix shape by TOKEN not line and at per-fix not per-pass granularity; the RANDOMISED post-convergence probe experiment with a pre-registered stopping rule; a post-cap probe on the next three cap-reached risky loops (3 agent runs, settles the cap question); extending `human_decision` to distinguish resume from waiver from send-back, plus the dismissal-recheck record of which ZERO exist; and either stopping the deletion of review files or requiring a machine-readable round-key header before deletion.

WHAT WOULD CHANGE THE RECOMMENDED CONSTANTS, recorded so a future session does not re-litigate from scratch: 20 more zero-event enforced rounds (0 of 42, upper bound 8.4 percent) would justify LOWERING the `risky` bar to 1; ANY SINGLE medium-or-worse finding in an enforced second round settles it at 2 PERMANENTLY; 35 zero-event probes puts the bound under 10 percent and 73 puts it under 5; and three converging post-cap probes out of five would justify raising the cap to 6.

STEP 93 IS HELD, AND `workflow-calibration` IS PULLED FORWARD AHEAD OF IT (HUMAN DECISIONS, 2026-07-31). THIS PARAGRAPH SUPERSEDES THE ESCALATION PARAGRAPH IMMEDIATELY BELOW IT, WHICH REMAINS ACCURATE ABOUT THE ARITHMETIC AND THE AUTHORISED FIX SET BUT IS NO LONGER THE NEXT ACTION. THE IMMEDIATE NEXT ACTION IS `workflow-calibration`'s DESIGN PASS, INCLUDING A GIT-HISTORY ARCHAEOLOGY. Step 93's escalation is NOT abandoned and NOT decided; it is DEFERRED PENDING THE CALIBRATION, with the branch `impl/checks-collision` held ENTIRELY UNTOUCHED at its fully reviewed state (no fix pass, no further rounds), so nothing decays and no agent runs are spent against a bar that is about to be measured. The step's three authorised fixes stay exactly as the round-4 triager built and measured them.

WHY THE HOLD IS NOT A STALL, and what would end it. Step 93's escalation reduces to ONE answerable question: IS A SINGLE VERIFICATION ROUND ENOUGH AFTER A DELETION-CLASS FIX PASS. This task has an unbroken FIVE-FOR-FIVE record that deletion-only fix passes re-seed nothing while prose-authoring passes manufacture the next round's finding, but five anecdotes is not a rate. Across the recoverable corpus it becomes one, and censoring bites far less here than on the general question because step 93's round WILL run, so the quantity in dispute is observable rather than counterfactual. If deletion-class passes show a near-zero injection rate corpus-wide, step 93's fix set (TWO OF THREE PURE DELETIONS) justifies one verification round on evidence rather than by concession; if they inject at a real rate, the `risky` bar is doing genuine work and step 93 gets its two rounds. Either way the escalation stops being a judgement call.

THE MEASUREMENTS THAT DROVE THIS, computed at the escalation from the 204 round records and reproducible from `docs/metrics/workflow.jsonl`. BY RISK CLASS: `low_risk` 57 artifacts, bar 1 clean, MEDIAN 2 rounds, mean excess 0.98, max 5; `risky` 22 artifacts, bar 2 clean, MEDIAN 4 rounds, mean excess 2.14, max 9, with FOUR OF 22 (18 percent) running past the cap of 5. SEVERITY MIX IS NEARLY IDENTICAL ACROSS CLASSES, `low_risk` 55 of 250 findings medium-or-worse (22 percent) against `risky` 43 of 169 (25 percent), so the extra rounds on risky artifacts are NOT producing junk and the "the loop nitpicks risky artifacts" reading is UNSUPPORTED. THE TWO RATES THAT MATTER: on an UNCHANGED artifact a re-review still finds something 3 of 21 times (14.3 percent, 95 percent CI 5.0 to 34.6), while on a CHANGED artifact it is 99 of 183 (54.1 percent, CI 46.9 to 61.2). So one clean round on an unchanged artifact is right about 86 percent of the time and two consecutive gets to about 98 percent IF THE ROUNDS ARE INDEPENDENT, which is the assumption most at risk. CONCLUSION THE DATA SUPPORTS: the 2-consecutive-clean bar is EXPENSIVE BY CONSTRUCTION, which is arithmetic and not miscalibration, and the CAP OF 5 IS INCONSISTENT WITH IT because it sits below where the distribution still has real mass.

THE QUESTION THE EXISTING DATA CANNOT ANSWER, WHICH IS WHY THE ARCHAEOLOGY IS OWED. That 54.1 percent has TWO INCOMPATIBLE EXPLANATIONS the log cannot separate: fix passes INJECT new defects, or changed content is GENUINELY NEW and unreviewed. THE REMEDY IS OPPOSITE IN EACH CASE, since under injection raising the cap makes things WORSE (more rounds, more fixes, more injections) and the fix is to constrain fix passes, while under new-content constraining fixes does nothing and the cap should rise. The round record has NO per-finding identity, NO causation link, NO finding class (prose versus code versus test) and NO record of fix-pass shape; the ledger template states "no cross-round finding identity" as a DELIBERATE design decision. The evidence exists only as prose in reviewer and triage files that are committed then DELETED at step close: 296 such files are recoverable from git history (`git log --diff-filter=D --name-only -- 'docs/plans/*.reviews/*'`), plus the 5 currently live, which is close to every review this project has ever run.

THE ARCHAEOLOGY AS DECIDED (human, 2026-07-31), and the ROLE QUESTION IT LEAVES OPEN. Run it as EXPLORERS with an analysis-specific discipline in the brief, writing under `docs/plans/workflow-calibration.explorations/`, rather than defining a new analyst role in the pack first; the reasoning is that every other role in this pack was adopted after the need RECURRED, not on first sight (Minimal by default), and this run is how we find out whether the discipline is genuinely different. THE HUMAN RAISED A REAL DISTINCTION WORTH KEEPING ON THE RECORD: an analyst's failure mode is SILENT MISCOUNTING, not bad judgement, so it is verified by re-derivation and reconciliation rather than by adversarial re-reading, and this project has already been burned by that failure mode THREE TIMES (the orchestrator's false "first waiver ever" from a grep that matched nothing, a word count recorded as 45-to-43 that was 46-to-42 and propagated through three agents, and a reviewer's 100/100 measurement that re-measured at 10/100). IF THE DISCIPLINE PROVES SUBSTANTIALLY DIFFERENT FROM AN EXPLORER'S, THAT IS THE EVIDENCE FOR ADDING THE ROLE, and it should be raised then rather than assumed now.

WHAT THE ARCHAEOLOGY MUST COLLECT, PER FINDING RATHER THAN PER ROUND, which is the whole point: round identity (step, increment, round number, commit range); finding id, severity AS RAISED, severity AFTER TRIAGE, and verdict (valid, accept-residual, dismissed); CLASS (prose, code, test, config); PROVENANCE, meaning whether the finding was introduced by a prior fix pass in the same loop and if so which round's; FIX-PASS SHAPE for the pass preceding the round (deletion-only versus authored, lines added versus deleted); reviewer attribution (model, lens); and whether the finding ESCAPED and was re-raised in a later round.

HOW ITS CORRECTNESS IS ENFORCED, strongest measure first. THE JSONL IS ALREADY AN ORACLE: every round record independently carries `valid_findings` and `severities` written at the time, so any per-finding extraction MUST RECONCILE TO THOSE NUMBERS EXACTLY across all 204 rounds, and an extraction that does not sum correctly is provably wrong before anyone interprets it. On top of that: TWO INDEPENDENT EXTRACTORS over the same corpus with disagreements treated as signal (the reviewer-diversity principle applied to data); STRUCTURED OUTPUT as TSV or JSON so the analysis is re-runnable rather than asserted (Reproducible, Structured data first); A SEPARATE ANALYST THAT NEVER SAW THE EXTRACTION, so extraction bias cannot leak into interpretation; random spot-checks re-derived from source; and PRE-REGISTERED QUESTIONS decided before looking, because n is small and the temptation to fish is real. ONE DESIGN POINT THAT MATTERS: PREFER TRIAGER VERDICTS OVER REVIEWER CLAIMS, because the findings files contain known errors that triagers caught, so extracting what was RULED rather than what was CLAIMED inherits the adjudication instead of the noise.

THE LIMIT TO HOLD THE ARCHAEOLOGY TO, stated in advance so no one over-reads its output: IT CANNOT FIX CENSORING. It records what rounds that RAN found, never what an unrun round would have found, so it can measure the fix-injection rate and the finding-class mix (both currently unknown) but CANNOT on its own establish the value of the second clean round beyond the 3-of-21 above. That needs a FORWARD EXPERIMENT, occasionally running one extra round past convergence and recording what it finds, which should be built into the calibration step rather than discovered later. The step's own sidecar already frames the clean-round constant as a CENSORING problem needing a sequential or survival framing rather than agreement statistics, and already names per-finding identity as "the enabling schema change" and "the first design question"; what it does NOT contain is the FIX-INJECTION HYPOTHESIS, which is this task's own discovery and must be added to its brief by the planner.

`F1-COLDREAD` (`medium`, VALID, FIX REQUIRED) IS THE ROUND'S REAL FINDING, and it is the one the fidelity lens structurally could not catch, because the change was faithful to a brief that was itself insufficient here. At `pack/prompts/orchestrator.md:31` the pre-existing second-person imperative "record the resolved answer as a durable Open-Questions decision" SURVIVED and the routing clause was appended BESIDE it, so the same sentence COMMANDS an act and ROUTES IT AWAY. Because the Open Questions queue IS `[[question]]` entries in the plan TOML, and because the Socratic path has no create-then-decide stage, recording a decided Socratic answer MINTS a `[[question]]`, which is exactly what `Q-67` reserves for the planner. An orchestrator can read "recording" and "the fold" as separate acts, author the `[[question]]` itself, and route only downstream `[[step]]` work, re-permitting the precise misstep this step exists to prevent.

THE TRIAGER REFUSED THE IMPLEMENTER'S COUNTER-ARGUMENT ON EVIDENCE, and the reasoning is worth keeping because both grounds looked plausible. The implementer had deliberately kept the verb, arguing that deleting it would move EVERY fold off the orchestrator including the trivial ones, and that it was matching the structure of `pack/AGENTS.md:43`. Ground one is a FALSE PREMISE: the scope constraint forbids an UNQUALIFIED "the planner authors that fold", so re-voicing the verb while keeping "non-trivial (authoring a `[[question]]` or a `[[step]]`)" verbatim moves no trivial fold anywhere; the implementer conflated "keep the imperative" with "keep trivial folds with the orchestrator", which are two separate choices. Ground two is FALSE AT THE DISPUTED POINT: `pack/AGENTS.md:43` uses three ACTOR-LESS verbs ("becomes", "routed", "edited in") while the prompt kept "record" as a SECOND-PERSON IMPERATIVE. The decisive evidence is structural: the Socratic mode at `:43` has NO create-then-decide stage, whereas the exploration mode at `:45` explicitly does ("records the question ... with status `exploring` ... only then does it move the item to `open`"); and `src/plan/source.rs:314-317` requires `folded_into` when a question is `decided`, so a Socratic recording mints a `[[question]]` AND must name a `[[step]]`. The step's own brief had also forbidden this outcome in advance at its lines 8 and 20. EVERY CITATION IN BOTH FINDINGS FILES REPRODUCED, with no misnumbering for the first time in several rounds.

THE TWO LENSES CONVERGED ON THE SAME DEFECT FROM DIFFERENT DIRECTIONS AND DIFFERENT MODELS, which is the strongest signal this task has produced and the clearest vindication of the diversity rule. A `claude-opus-5` REGION SWEEP and a `claude-sonnet-5` MUTATION HUNT independently found `claim_dir`'s OWN error arm at `src/checks.rs:452` to be executed by no test, and the triager confirmed them ONE finding rather than assuming it: same line, byte-identical mutation, same fixture shape, same cause. THE CAUSE IS THE PREVIOUS ROUND'S OWN FIX: round 3's `AD1a` pinned the CALLER's `map_err` through the injected `claim` seam, and the seam BY CONSTRUCTION never calls the real `claim_dir`, so the coverage reads complete one level up while the arm one level down stays bare. The triager's own grep found `claim_dir` has exactly TWO test-side call sites, both inside `a_directory_claim_is_exclusive`, driving `Ok(true)` and `Ok(false)`, with NOTHING driving `Err`. Both mutations survive at 386 green and clippy 0/0, and a binary A/B against a `chmod 555` TMPDIR shows the committed build reporting `Permission denied (os error 13)` while the mutant reports `could not reserve a unique runner worktree directory after 16 attempts`, the exact misdiagnosis round 3 believed it had closed.

ROUND 4 VERDICTS. `RG1`/`MU1` (`medium`, VALID, fix required), deduplicated as above. `RG2` (`low`, VALID, fix required, DELETION, SITE LIST EXTENDED BY THE TRIAGER): the seam's doc sentence claims "Every real claim in this repository WINS", falsified deterministically by instrumenting the lost-claim arm, exactly 1 real lost claim per whole-suite run over 5/5 runs at `a_directory_claim_is_exclusive:1705`. THE TRIAGER FOUND THE IDENTICAL FALSE CLAIM A SECOND TIME at `src/checks.rs:1712-1713`, seven lines below a comment asserting the opposite, and noted that fixing only the cited site WOULD HAVE RE-SEEDED ROUND 5. `RG3` (`low`, VALID BUT ACCEPT RESIDUAL): the guard-before-add ordering is unpinned; the region reviewer built a 78-line integration test and argued to accept on COST, and the triager REFUTED that ground (the cheapest form is 40 lines folded into the existing `tests/checks_missing_tmpdir.rs` with no new binary, cheaper than the already-declined `AD1b`) then accepted anyway on VALUE, which is the honest version of the same verdict. `RG4` (`low`, VALID, fix required): Invariant B over-claims that widening the prune by prefix would close the window, which cannot reach an orphan recorded under a DIFFERENT `TMPDIR`.

`RG2` ALSO CAUGHT A TRIAGER ERROR IN THE PREVIOUS ROUND, and it is the non-zero twin of a lesson this project already recorded against the orchestrator. Round 3's triage certified the sentence TRUE at `triage-r3.md:297` using `grep -n "reserve_runner_worktree"`, a pattern that CANNOT MATCH the direct `claim_dir` call that falsifies it, and whose own output contains the concurrent site `:1826` contradicting the sentence's "sequentially". `git log -S` establishes the falsifying test landed at `339d26a` in round 1 while the sentence was authored at `14692f3` in round 2, SO THE SENTENCE WAS FALSE THE DAY IT WAS WRITTEN. The recorded orchestrator lesson was "a grep returning zero is evidence about the PATTERN as much as about the repo"; this is its non-zero twin, A GREP RETURNING HITS PROVES ONLY THAT THE PATTERN MATCHED, NOT THAT THE POPULATION IT WAS MEANT TO STAND FOR WAS COVERED.

THE MINIMAL FIX SET, all built, measured and reverted by the triager: `23 insertions, 12 deletions` in `src/checks.rs`, ZERO production lines, NO new test binary, green at 386 with clippy 0/0 and RED under both `:452` mutations. TWO OF THE THREE ARE PURE DELETIONS. (1) `RG1`/`MU1`: the REGION reviewer's form ADOPTED OVER the mutation reviewer's on measured grounds, 13 lines folded into the existing `a_directory_claim_is_exclusive` against 22 (advertised as "about 18"), no new fixture-name class, and it completes the contract of a test whose own comment already says "Both outcomes matter"; the rejected form adds a 19th fixture-name class that LEAKS on every RED run, measured at 4 leftovers from 4 RED runs. (2) `RG2`: pure deletion at BOTH sites, zero words authored; the triager DECLINED the region reviewer's replacement wording because it authors a fresh claim naming another test, the class this task has measured re-seeding five times, and the surviving sentence at `:1711` already carries the justification. (3) `RG4`: one narrowing clause, the ONLY authored prose in the set, with pure deletion rejected because that sentence uniquely carries the Principle 18 least-authority reason the module declines to widen the prune, which the routed-out step inherits.

THE CLOCK-RATE NON-FINDING WAS ARBITRATED AND THE REGION REVIEWER'S RESTRAINT WAS RIGHT. It measured 0 of 100000 duplicates at two threads against the step brief's 8.7 percent and explicitly DECLINED to raise it, citing this task's four-for-four prescription record. The triager reproduced BOTH: a tight-loop method gives 6.88/7.43/8.73 percent at two threads and 51.5/55.0/67.1 at sixteen, matching the brief, while a per-round barrier gives 0.00 and 0.8 to 2.1 percent, matching the reviewer. Two different experiments, not a conflict; the brief's figures STAND and no record correction is due. This is the first time on this task a reviewer's self-restraint was tested and upheld rather than a prescription being overturned.

ORCHESTRATOR DEFECT (5), recorded like the other four. THE TWO ROUND-4 REVIEWER WORKTREES WERE CREATED NESTED INSIDE THE IMPLEMENTER'S WORKTREE, at `.claude/worktrees/impl-checks-collision/.claude/worktrees/rev93-r4-{a,b}` instead of at the top level, because a `cd` from an earlier Bash call PERSISTED and the `git worktree add` used a RELATIVE path. This is the same shell-cwd-persistence hazard recorded earlier in this task, recurring in a new place. NO HARM RESULTED and that was verified rather than assumed: both worktrees were genuine isolated checkouts of the correct commit `3f49012` on their own branches, `.claude/` is gitignored so the implementer's `git status` stayed empty throughout, and its tree was confirmed byte-clean at `3f49012` after removal. THE CURE, adopted: ALWAYS pass an ABSOLUTE path to `git worktree add`, never a relative one, since a relative path silently resolves against whatever cwd a previous call left behind.

STEP 93 WORK-REVIEW ROUND 3 (2026-07-30, record 230, outcome `new_valid`, 3 raw findings from one lens becoming 4 after a triager split, NOTHING dismissed, severities 1 `medium` + 3 `low`, no high or critical so NO backstop; streak STILL 0 of the 2 its `risky` class needs). Implementer commit `6a726ed`, `src/checks.rs` plus a new `tests/checks_missing_tmpdir.rs`, +175/-9; 385 tests pass, clippy silent, independently reconfirmed by the orchestrator and by both reviewers and the triager.

THIS IS THE FIRST ROUND IN THIS TASK RUN ON TWO DIFFERENT MODELS, correcting the diversity violation recorded against the orchestrator, and THE SPLIT IS THE HEADLINE: the `claude-sonnet-5` fix-verification lens returned ZERO findings, the `claude-opus-5` adversarial lens returned THREE. The triager ruled the zero CORRECT on the three authorised fixes (it reproduced the decisive disclosed-item mutation itself and did not disturb that ruling) but INCOMPLETE on its own "manufacture nothing new" clause, and diagnosed the shape of the gap precisely: the two misses are one ASSERTION three lines below the one it ruled on, and one CLAUSE of the sentence whose other clause it ruled on, so its sweep was over SENTENCES rather than over the region. That is a lens-scope defect, not a model defect, and it is the first evidence this task has that the earlier all-opus rounds were narrower than they were presented as. DO NOT read this as "sonnet is weaker": the fix-verification lens was pointed at three named fixes and answered that question correctly and in depth, including ruling independently on a disclosed self-referential assertion and getting it right.

ROUND 3 VERDICTS. `AD1` was SPLIT by the triager because the halves differ in consequence and in fix cost by an order of magnitude. `AD1a` (`medium`, VALID, fix required): the reservation's claim ERROR arm is executed by no test. The triager confirmed the reviewer's two mutations (`unwrap_or(false)` and bare `?`, both GREEN at 385) but RE-GROUNDED the severity on a mutation NEITHER LENS TRIED, `claim(&path).unwrap_or(true)`, also GREEN at 385, under which the reservation returns a path whose claim was never established, falsifying `src/checks.rs:483-485` ("that outcome, not an entropy argument, is what makes the returned path exclusively ours"). The reviewer's own stated ground, message quality, would have carried only `low`. This is the same class as round 2's `X2` and is NOT covered by it: `X2` pinned a LOST claim and says nothing about an ERRORED one, and round 2's table recorded this arm as not tried. `AD1b` (`low`, VALID BUT ACCEPT RESIDUAL): the temp-dir creation error arm degrades by one noun while both messages still name the path and the same errno and exit 2; pinning it costs a whole new spawned-binary integration test because `std::env::temp_dir()` has no seam, and injecting the temp dir is the second-testability-parameter move round 2 already declined for the clock. `AD2` (`low`, VALID, fix required, FRAMING CORRECTED): `message.contains(&RUNNER_RESERVE_ATTEMPTS.to_string())` can be satisfied by digits from the path itself. The reviewer's 100/100 spurious-pass figure DID NOT REPRODUCE; the triager re-measured 10 PASS / 90 FAIL over 100 fresh processes and found the digits arriving from the 19-digit nanos rather than the pid, so the assertion does not pin nothing, it pins at a rate that is a property of the developer's pid counter and clock digits, 0 percent on the reviewer's machine and 87 to 90 percent on the triager's. `AD3` (`low`, VALID, fix required, REMEDY CHANGED TO DELETION): the comment written to fix round 2's `X8b` states in the present tense that nothing exercises the lost-claim verdict, the retry, or the exhaustion error and that each can be deleted with a green suite; all three measured false against the tree the comment sits in.

THE MINIMAL FIX SET, all three built, measured and reverted by the triager: 386 passed, clippy 0/0, TEN consecutive full `cargo test` runs green, +31/-4 in `src/checks.rs`, and ZERO PRODUCTION CODE CHANGED. Fix A (`AD1a`): one 27-line unit test on the EXISTING seam, RED under all three claim-error mutations. Fix B (`AD2`): one line, `contains(&RUNNER_RESERVE_ATTEMPTS.to_string())` -> `contains(&format!("after {RUNNER_RESERVE_ATTEMPTS} attempts"))`, which moves the measured kill rate from 10/100 to 100/100 while keeping the symbolic constant so the settled `16 -> 3` behaviour is unchanged. Fix C (`AD3`): DELETE the false clause, net -2 lines, ZERO words authored.

PRESCRIPTION TESTED NOT TRUSTED, FOURTH INSTANCE, and this one cut in three directions rather than the usual one. The `AD1` fix was advertised by its author as two tests, 77 lines, 387 passing; BUILT, it is ONE test, 27 lines, 386 passing, and it kills a mutation the reviewer never had, with the reviewer's second test measured as disproportionate and declined. `AD2`'s proposal was RIGHT IN SUBSTANCE but was unbuilt by its author and its headline measurement did not reproduce. `AD3`'s diagnosis was right and its METHOD was wrong: the reviewer prescribed a past-tense rewrite, which the triager declined explicitly as re-seeding, choosing deletion instead. The running tally is now `FN-2` (step 92), round 1's `T1`, round 2's clock parameter, and all three of round 3's.

STEP 93 WORK-REVIEW ROUND 2 (2026-07-30, record 229, outcome `new_valid`, 8 raw findings surviving as 8 after one merge and one split, NOTHING dismissed, severities 3 `medium` + 5 `low`, no high or critical so NO backstop; streak STILL 0 of the 2 its `risky` class needs). ALL FOUR ROUND-1 FIXES LANDED, each verified by mutation rather than by reading. Neither lens could break the uniqueness property, and neither could make the prune reclaim a LIVE worktree.

THE ROUND'S THROUGH-LINE, MEASURED BY THE TRIAGER AND STRONGER THAN EITHER LENS PUT IT: FOUR OF FIVE LOAD-BEARING PARTS CAN EACH BE DELETED ALONE WITH 382 TESTS GREEN AND CLIPPY SILENT, namely the `create_dir_all(&temp)`, the atomic sequence, the `if claimed` verdict, and the retry loop. The suite goes red only when the whole construction goes. TWO RECORDED EXPLANATIONS ARE THEREFORE FALSE AND ARE CORRECTED HERE: the implementer's "the 16 retries absorb the collisions" and round 1's "the retry path carries the property". Instrumentation shows deepest retry depth 1 and 2020 attempts for 2000 reservations WITH AND WITHOUT the layer-1 mutation, so there are ZERO collisions and ZERO retries; with layer 1 disabled AND a panic planted on the lost-claim branch, `checks::` passes 30/30 three times. The property test's redness is carried by the `mkdir` syscall's CLOCK SPACING, not by either layer, and the margin is thin (1 to 15 duplicates per 2000, red 8 of 8).

A THIRD UNSOUND REVIEWER PRESCRIPTION, CAUGHT THE SAME WAY. The verification lens proposed a clock parameter plus a frozen-clock test and claimed it would also close the retry hole. The triager BUILT IT AND MEASURED: green on committed code, RED under `fetch_add` -> `load`, but GREEN under `RUNNER_RESERVE_ATTEMPTS = 1` and GREEN under `if claimed || true`, so its advertised side-benefit is FALSE, because on correct code a frozen clock never loses a claim. The adversarial lens's injectable-claim prescription does work as described and was adopted instead. THAT IS THREE FOR THREE IN THIS TASK: `FN-2` (step 92), the round-1 `T1` proposal, and now this. THE STANDING LESSON, now beyond doubt: A REVIEWER'S DIAGNOSIS AND ITS PRESCRIPTION CARRY DIFFERENT WEIGHT, and a prescription must be executed and measured before it is trusted.

ROUND 2 VERDICTS. `X2` (`medium`, VALID, = adversarial F2 merged with the retry half of verification F1): the loop's collision handling is executed by NOTHING; `if claimed` -> `if claimed || true` at `:524` leaves the full suite, the new exclusivity test AND the property test all green. This is the unclosed remainder of round 1's `T1`. `X3` (`medium`, VALID): the round-1 `TMPDIR` regression fix is pinned by no test; deleting `:501-506` leaves the suite green while `TMPDIR=<missing>` goes exit 0 -> exit 2. `X4` (`medium`, VALID for its PROSE half only): the prune permanently SKIPS A REGISTERED DEAD ORPHAN when git records a symlink-resolved path outside `std::env::temp_dir()`, and both prune tests FAIL under a symlinked `TMPDIR`; the MECHANISM IS PRE-EXISTING (prune body byte-identical `HEAD~2..HEAD`) but the Invariant B text THIS step newly wrote states the bound as registration only, which is incomplete. `X1` (`low`, ACCEPT RESIDUAL WITH THE REASON CORRECTED): layer 1 is a NAME OPTIMISATION and correctness rides on layer 2, which fix 2 pins end to end; the previously recorded reason was wrong and must not be repeated. `X5`, `X6` (`low`, ACCEPT RESIDUAL), `X7` (`low`, record correction only: the commit message quotes an errno the binary does not produce), `X8` (`low`, VALID, two comments this commit wrote are false).

THE AUTHORISED ROUND-2 FIX SET, all three implemented, measured and reverted by the triager (385 green, clippy silent). (1) `X3`: an integration test, NO production change, about 55 lines, green on HEAD and RED with `:501-506` deleted. (2) `X2` + `X8b`: `reserve_runner_worktree_with(pid, claim: impl Fn(&Path) -> io::Result<bool>)`, about 8 production lines plus two tests, RED under `|| true` and RED under attempts=1, with `fetch_add` -> `load` staying green as the DOCUMENTED residual `X1`. (3) `X4` prose + `X8a`: two clauses, Invariant B only (`:42-51`) plus `:495-496`; do NOT touch `:336-339` or `:874-877`, and do NOT widen the prune.

A NEW ROADMAP STEP IS OWED, and it is PLANNER work not yet done: close `X4`'s CODE half and `X6` together, since `std::env::temp_dir()` is consumed raw at `:494` and `:573`. It must canonicalise the prune's gate and reject a relative temp dir, and it NEEDS ITS OWN RISK CLASS because it changes what the prune deletes. The triager endorsed the reviewer's split of prose-here / code-later, and ruled that the tempting "but the suite lies" counter does not carry, since the environment dependence is pre-existing and orthogonal to this step.

FILESYSTEM HYGIENE, worth recording because it is a real cost of adversarial review: a round-2 reviewer's exhaustion-path mutation left 32272 empty `agent-scaffold-checks-run-*` directories in `/tmp` and its own cleanup was refused by the permission classifier, so the ORCHESTRATOR removed them. The round-2 triager was briefed to point its probes at a scratch `TMPDIR` and reported creating 0 in `/tmp`. FUTURE REVIEWER BRIEFS ON THIS STEP MUST CARRY THAT INSTRUCTION.

STEP 93 WORK-REVIEW ROUND 1 (2026-07-30, record 228, outcome `new_valid`, 12 raw findings deduplicated to 10, 8 VALID and 2 DISMISSED, severities 3 `medium` + 5 `low`; NO high or critical raised or dismissed so NO backstop owed; streak 0 of the 2 its `risky` class needs). Implementer commit `b890c4a`, `src/checks.rs` only, +212/-26. IT CHOSE (a)+(d) AND MEASURED THE CHOICE rather than arguing it: an 8-process probe at the constant-pid fixture template gave (a)-alone duplicates in 8 of 8 runs and (a)+(d) zero, with `create_dir` actually rejecting a name twice in 80000, so layer 2 is not decorative. THE UNIQUENESS PROPERTY ITSELF HOLDS and both lenses verified it independently; the fix set below is about COVERAGE AND EDGES, not about the fix being wrong.

THE RED-3 MUTATION VINDICATED THE `risky` CLASSIFICATION. Prepending the disambiguator instead of appending it (`{seq}-{pid}-{nanos}`) breaks `owning_pid` parsing and makes the startup prune RECLAIM A LIVE WORKTREE, confirmed independently by the implementer, the evidence lens and the triager. The evidence lens found it STRONGER than reported: run in isolation a third prune test also fails, so the implementer's "one prune test still passed" was an artifact of which sequence values a run draws, not a property. That is precisely the destructive failure the classification was made for, and the fix now carries a named test pinning the pid's position.

BOTH LENSES INDEPENDENTLY FOUND THE SAME CENTRAL DEFECT, which is the strongest signal this round produced: NEITHER LAYER OF THE FIX IS PINNED BY A TEST (`T1`, `medium`, RAISED from the evidence lens's `low`). All four mutations reproduce: `create_dir` -> `create_dir_all` DESTROYS EXCLUSIVITY yet the whole suite stays green; the `AlreadyExists` retry arm is NEVER EXECUTED ANYWHERE, proved by panic-probing it and staying green; `fetch_add` -> `load` (a constant sequence) stays green 3 of 3; and deleting the reservation is caught only INCIDENTALLY by a cleanup `.unwrap()` whose message says nothing about reservations. So the suite pinned "at least one disambiguator survives", not "both layers do", in a step whose entire purpose is establishing those properties.

THE TRIAGER DISPROVED THE ADVERSARIAL REVIEWER'S OWN PRESCRIBED FIX, BY IMPLEMENTING IT. The reviewer proposed a 3-line test (assert the reserved path `is_dir()`, then a second `fs::create_dir` returns `AlreadyExists`) and claimed it would kill three mutations. The triager wrote that exact test and measured: it passes on clean code, and UNDER THE `create_dir_all` MUTATION THE WHOLE `checks::` MODULE IS STILL GREEN (30 passed, 0 failed), because it tests `std` rather than the production site and never drives the retry arm. Implementing it as written would have produced a GREEN ROUND THAT PINS NOTHING. This is the SECOND time in this task that a reviewer's proposed remedy was unsound and the triager caught it by MEASURING rather than reasoning; the first was step 92's `FN-2`. RECORD THE LESSON: a reviewer's diagnosis and a reviewer's prescription carry different weight, and the prescription must be executed and measured before it is trusted.

THE AUTHORISED FIX SET, four fixes all in `src/checks.rs`, verified single-site by the triager. (1) `T1` (`medium`): extract `claim_dir(path) -> io::Result<bool>` (7 lines, called from the reservation loop) plus a 4-line exclusivity test; the triager MEASURED this green on `b890c4a`, RED under `create_dir_all`, and RED under a panic in the taken branch, so it kills the mutations the reviewer's version left alive. (2) `T2` (`medium`, a REAL BEHAVIOURAL REGRESSION the evidence lens missed): `fs::create_dir` rather than `create_dir_all` turns a `TMPDIR` naming a missing directory from WORKING into a HARD FAILURE, because `git worktree add` used to create the leading directories, and the error lost all context (`No such file or directory (os error 2)` against the pre-fix `WorktreeSetup` message naming the operation and full path); fix by `create_dir_all(&temp)` before the loop with path-carrying error context, and correct the comment claiming the reservation "changes nothing downstream", which is false. Fixes 1 and 2 land in one edit. (3) `T3` (`medium`): Module Invariant B at `:37-41` and two comments restating it at `:324-326` and `:816-817` are NOW FALSE, because a SIGKILL between `create_dir` and `worktree add` leaves an UNREGISTERED directory the prune cannot see; qualify all three, and do NOT widen the prune to sweep the temp dir, which would give it authority over other repositories' runner directories. (4) `T6` (`low`): move two `fs::remove_dir` calls above the assertion loop so a failing fixture does not leak. ACCEPTED RESIDUALS, do not re-raise: `T4` (a bypass at the production call site stays green; the brief already settled that the grep is the proportional evidence), `T5` (the `WorktreeGuard`-before-add move is unpinned; pinning it needs failure injection into `run()`, disproportionate), `T7`, `T10`. DISMISSED: `T8`, `T9`. ONE OFF-BY-ONE citation was found in a findings file (`:439-440` where the sentence is at `:440-441`), not a dismissal ground.

HUMAN DECIDED (2026-07-30) where the three `agent-scaffold next` defects go: FOLD THEM INTO GATE 4, the validation-constraints step, rather than giving them their own step, patching only the missing increment, or deferring them past the rename. Ground: the false-completion bug is a false green of the same family as `Q-55`, and its fix IS a constraint (a step's declared `[[step.increment]]` set should cover what its own brief commits to, and `next` must not assert completion from a converged last increment alone), so one planner pass covers both and the rename gates stay at three. This is a scheduling sub-decision, so no `[[question]]` and no receipt.

STEP 93 `checks-runner-worktree-name-collision` WORK LOOP OPENED (2026-07-30), GATE 1 OF 3 FOR THE RENAME. RISK CLASS = `risky`, so 2 consecutive clean rounds converge. CLASSIFIED BY THE ORCHESTRATOR AT LOOP-OPEN per its duty under the convergence rule, and stated to the human rather than put to them, because the rule assigns this call to the orchestrator. GROUNDS, and note they cut AGAINST the "this project over-classes prose work" calibration recorded for step 90: this is CODE, not prose, and the failure mode runs through `WorktreeGuard`'s `remove_dir_all` on a shared path (`src/checks.rs:329-342`) plus a startup prune that decides what to reclaim by parsing the owning pid out of the name (`owning_pid`, `src/checks.rs:400-405`). A wrong name format can therefore make the prune RECLAIM A LIVE WORKTREE, which is destructive and not cheaply reversible, and that is the definition of risky the rule gives. The artifact is EXACTLY: `src/checks.rs` (the shared name generator, the `run()` call site, the three fixtures at `:1462`/`:1491`/`:1492`, and the four comments the brief names at `:72-77`, `:400-402`, `:789-790`, `:845-847`) plus the new property test. If it grows beyond that, the classification gets revisited EXPLICITLY. THE STEP NEEDS AN IMPLEMENTER, NOT A PLANNER: its plan review already converged over 4 rounds. THE ONE GENUINE FORK the brief leaves open is which candidate fix to take, (a) an atomic counter, (d) a `create_dir` reservation, or the (a)+(d) composition; the brief instructs the implementer to pick and ARGUE it, or to raise it if it is a real fork, so it is deliberately NOT pre-decided here and will come to the human only if the implementer raises it.

STEP 90 IS COMPLETE, CONVERGED AND MERGED (2026-07-30). ROUND 2 (record 227) was CLEAN: 4 findings, ALL `low`, 3 ruled VALID BUT ACCEPT RESIDUAL and 1 DISMISSED, so NO fix was required and the `low_risk` bar of 1 clean round is met. One dismissal at `low`, so no backstop re-check was owed. Fix commit `3285320` was proved BYTE-EXACT by reconstruction rather than eyeballing (applying the authorised substitution to the old line yields the actual new line under full string equality; 34 lines both sides with line 31 the only difference; branches 1 and 3 byte-identical; zero internal semicolons; the branch split still yields exactly 3 parts). FF-merged; the merged product diff is exactly FIVE files (`pack/AGENTS.md`, `pack/prompts/orchestrator.md`, and the three deployed copies); post-merge 379 tests pass with the flake not firing; `diff pack/prompts/orchestrator.md .agents/prompts/orchestrator.md` empty. Status flipped to `complete` and `[[step.increment]] decision-folder-currency-inc1` (`low_risk`) declared.

ACCEPTED RESIDUALS FROM STEP 90, DO NOT RE-RAISE. (1) The step's own brief at `decision-folder-currency.md:30` and `:38` claims `.agents/prompts/` has NO whole-file drift guard; STALE since step 92 landed first and `src/agents_md_drift.rs:415` now guards that path. Over-warns rather than under-warns; the sidecar goes historical at task close. This VINDICATES the human's build-order decision, which sequenced 92 before 90 exactly so this regeneration would be enforced rather than trusted. (2) `pack/prompts/orchestrator.md:31`, `:33` and `pack/AGENTS.md:63` drop the "as above" pointer that `pack/AGENTS.md:41`, `:43` and `:71` carry. The re-enters-plan-review obligation is NOT lost: `pack/AGENTS.md:89` binds every writer unconditionally and `pack/prompts/orchestrator.md:29` states it two paragraphs above `:31`. The brief scoped these edits to the ACTOR CLAUSE only. (3) The rule is now hand-copied at SEVEN sites across two files with no guard between them, since the drift guards compare pack to deployed and not site to site. TWO FACTS THIS ESTABLISHED, recorded as a candidate against `Q-60`'s single-sourcing directive rather than a new step: the seven sites are DELIBERATELY non-identical (the brief calls the two ledger copies "counterparts, not duplicates"), so single-sourcing is a design question and not a mechanical extraction; and the fragment mechanism CANNOT currently reach the prompt at all, because `pack/pack.toml:104-107` declares `prompts/orchestrator.md` WITHOUT `render = true` while `:98-102` declares `AGENTS.md` WITH it, and `src/manifest.rs:533` renders only when that flag is set, so a `{{...}}` slot in the prompt would SHIP LITERALLY. The triager advised AGAINST minting a step now because the `Q-69` design pass will rewrite three of the seven sites; that advice is accepted. (4) A DISMISSED finding, recorded so it is not re-raised: the claim that the narrowed checkpoint gloss leaves the compaction-prep case with no legal actor. Dismissed because `pack/AGENTS.md:71`, `:99` and `:61` are BYTE-IDENTICAL to before the step, so step 90 copied an already-shipped gloss and removed a wrong licence rather than opening a hole.

AN ORCHESTRATOR BOOKKEEPING SLIP, corrected: this ledger recorded the round-1 fix as "45 words to 43" when the authorised strings are 46 and 42. The triager, the implementer and the orchestrator all repeated the same hand estimate, and the round-2 verification lens caught it; two independent recounts confirm 46/42. Corrected in place above. THE PATTERN IS THE SAME ONE ALREADY RECORDED TWICE THIS TASK: a number was passed along and restated as fact without anyone recomputing it.

THREE DEFECTS IN `agent-scaffold next`, FOUND BY ACTUALLY RUNNING IT (2026-07-30), and the orchestrator's own dogfooding gap that hid them. THE GAP FIRST: the orchestrator had been reading the plan TOML directly with grep and awk and had NOT been running `next` at all, so none of this was visible until the human asked whether the tool was still being dogfooded. Running the tool you are building is not optional; reading its source data by hand routes around exactly the surface a user depends on. (1) `next` RECOMMENDS A FALSE COMPLETION, the most serious of the three. It reports `workflow-driver / workflow-driver-stage1 ... state: converged, streak: 2/2 -> mark-step-complete` with the advice "mark the step complete, re-render, and commit". That advice is WRONG: `docs/plans/agent-scaffold.steps/workflow-driver.md:15` states "The committed near-term path is 0a -> 0b -> 1 -> 2", and `:20` describes Stage 2 (the typed `src/driver/` FSM engine) as committed near-term work that is NOT BUILT. The step is correctly `in-progress`. The mechanism of the defect: `next` infers completion from THE LAST DECLARED INCREMENT CONVERGING, and the plan declares only `stage0a`, `stage0b`, `stage1` as `[[step.increment]]` entries while Stage 2 is committed ONLY IN THE SIDECAR PROSE, with no structured entry. So the structured source is INCOMPLETE relative to the prose, and `next` reads the structured source and concludes the work is done. Had the orchestrator followed the advice it would have marked a step complete over committed unbuilt work, which is a FALSE GREEN of exactly the class the rename is being gated on, emitted by the tool's own advisory surface. (2) `next` OUTPUT IS 79503 BYTES, because it dumps the ledger RESUME STATE verbatim; the previously recorded measurement was 150145 bytes of which 799 was live, so the defect is smaller but unfixed and still unusable. This is the failure `resume-state-currency-signal` exists for, gated behind `Q-58`. (3) `next` WITH NO `--source` reports "no plan source" and "no plan steps found" and exits calmly, rather than discovering the plan or failing loudly, which is false-green-adjacent and rhymes with `Q-55`. `RD4-1` (the open step-92 finding): the GUARDED SET's self-extension sentence says an `[[asset]]` row added to `pack/pack.toml` under the prefix "is guarded with no edit here", but the true sufficient condition is a row the PINNED render emits, and a module-tagged row is not. THE DOMINANT LESSON OF THIS TASK, established independently in BOTH loops: EVERY FIX PASS THAT AUTHORS PROSE MANUFACTURES THE NEXT ROUND'S FINDING. Step 92 rounds 1 and 2 were authoring passes (47 lines added, then 68 rewritten) and both re-seeded; round 3 was constrained to DELETION-ONLY and manufactured NONE. Step 93 rounds 1 and 2 wrote MORE than the verdict prescribed and all the surplus broke; round 3 was constrained to exactly two deletions with a mechanically checkable diff shape (two files, two hunks) and an explicit escape valve ("if you see something else wrong, REPORT it, do not fix it"), and the planner used the valve twice. GOING FORWARD: constrain fix passes to deletions and single-token reverts where possible, give the writer a mechanically checkable diff shape, and always give it somewhere to put the improvement impulse other than the file.

ORCHESTRATOR DEFECTS THIS SESSION, recorded so they are not repeated. (1) I imposed a "property 4" invariant banning the words every/only/authoritative/exhaustive/all outside the COVERAGE block. That word list cannot distinguish a QUANTIFIER from a LOGICAL CONNECTIVE, and it caused a correct one-directional "normalize equal ONLY WHEN" to be rewritten as a false biconditional ("just when"). AMENDED, and the amendment is now live: the rule is SEMANTIC (no COVERAGE CLAIM outside the block); the word list is a CLASSIFICATION AID only and must NEVER trigger a rewrite; logical connectives in mechanical statements are EXEMPT; and the single expected exempt hit (`only when` at the `normalize_wrapping` site) is RECORDED so a reviewer does not read it as a regression. (2) My step-92 consolidation brief FAILED TO CARRY FORWARD a requirement round 2's triage had explicitly set ("narrow this text in the same pass"), which surfaced later as finding `RD-2`. A valid verdict lost in hand-off is exactly what the findings-file rule exists to prevent. (3) Earlier defects already recorded above: risk-class drift, triager worktrees built from the wrong base, and a ledger edit that rotted four line citations.

Isolation via `git stash create` (working-tree state, or HEAD when clean) -> `git worktree add --detach` under the system temp dir; a `WorktreeGuard` Drop removes it on every path; a startup self-healing prune reclaims orphans from killed runs. Round 4 (sonnet, fresh holistic, all four invariants re-verified): CLEAN, streak 2 -> CONVERGED. 149 tests, clippy clean throughout. `optional-modules` increment 2 SUB-INCREMENT 2c-ii (`--staged` + the pre-commit hook) review (CONVERGED via HUMAN DECISION at the round cap, RISKY, 5 rounds; worktree-isolated). Task `optional-modules-inc2cii`, artifact: `--staged` (index-tree isolation via `git write-tree` -> `commit-tree` -> the shared worktree runner), the inert `.agents/hooks/pre-commit` asset (delegates to `agent-scaffold checks --staged`; off-PATH SKIP with a note, human/reviewer-endorsed), the `scaffold --with-precommit-hook` create-if-absent install, and a new `executable` manifest field. Built by an isolated implementer in worktree `impl/inc2cii-staged-hook`. The implementer self-caught a LOAD-BEARING bug via the functional smoke (Principle 6): git sets `GIT_DIR`/`GIT_INDEX_FILE` inside a pre-commit hook, which broke `git worktree add` in the exact hook `--staged` exists for; fixed by stripping the git env from every runner call. Classified RISKY (writes into `.git/hooks`, index isolation, the never-clobber guarantee). This artifact was HARD: the adversarial loop found FOUR distinct write-escapes across rounds, each a variant of the same class, and each fixed. Round 1 (opus+sonnet+separate triager): 9 valid (1 med + 8 low, incl. F1 a dangling-symlink write-through via `exists()`-follows-symlink, the missing `executable` doc, the initial-commit staged test gap); sonnet F-3 (delegate `set -eu`) triager-DISPROVED empirically. Fix `6e9359b` (lstat never-clobber, `HookInstall::Skipped` so install errors never fail the scaffold, tests). Round 2 (opus+sonnet): opus found a HIGH, `--with-precommit-hook` into a SUBDIRECTORY of an ancestor repo installed a broken hook into the ANCESTOR's `.git/hooks/` (repo-root guard added, `9ca7ca9`). Round 3 (opus): another HIGH of the same class, a git LINKED WORKTREE escaped (shared hooks in the main repo); the fix generalised the guard to the load-bearing invariant, the RESOLVED hooks dir must be inside the output dir (`ff45a25`), which subsumed the whole class. Round 4 (opus, exhaustive attack across every git layout): the write-escape class CONFIRMED CLOSED; one new LOW (R4-1, a preview/action mismatch for a fresh-init inheriting a global `core.hooksPath` outside the dir). At this point the artifact had run 4 rounds without a clean one (each found progressively less-severe valid issues, high->high->low), reaching the 5-round total-cap. ESCALATED to the human per the cap contract (options + recommendation, ledger as evidence). Human DECISION: fix R4-1, run ONE clean confirming round, accept, matching ceremony to the now-reduced stakes since the dangerous class is closed and independently confirmed. R4-1 fixed with an honest conditional preview label (`e101539`). Round 5 (sonnet, fresh): CLEAN, streak 1. CONVERGED by the human decision (not the default 2 clean rounds for risky). 168 tests, clippy clean throughout. MERGE: fast-forwarded to `e101539` (main unchanged during the step). Rounds logged (records 78-83, incl. the `escalation` record with `human_decision: decision`).
